\displaystyle\left|\boldsymbol{f}_{1}^{\top}\left[\left(\frac{\mathbf{x}_{t-1}}{n}\otimes\mathbf{1}_{m}\right)\odot\left(\mathbf{K}_{t,\boldsymbol{\eta}(\mathbf{x}_{t-1})}-\mathbf{K}_{t,\boldsymbol{\eta}(\boldsymbol{\nu}_{t-1})}\right)\right]\boldsymbol{f}_{2}\right|

(B.55)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left|\left(\boldsymbol{f}_{1}\odot\frac{\mathbf{x}_{t-1}}{n}\right)^{\top}\left(\mathbf{K}_{t,\boldsymbol{\eta}(\mathbf{x}_{t-1})}-\mathbf{K}_{t,\boldsymbol{\eta}(\boldsymbol{\nu}_{t-1})}\right)\boldsymbol{f}_{2}\right|

(B.56)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle c\|\boldsymbol{f}_{1}\|_{\infty}\|\boldsymbol{f}_{2}\|_{\infty}\left\|\boldsymbol{\eta}(\mathbf{x}_{t-1})-\boldsymbol{\eta}(\boldsymbol{\nu}_{t-1})\right\|_{\infty}

(B.57)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle c\|\boldsymbol{f}_{1}\|_{\infty}\|\boldsymbol{f}_{2}\|_{\infty}\left\|\frac{\mathbf{x}_{t-1}}{n}-\boldsymbol{\nu}_{t-1}\right\|_{\infty}

(B.58)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle c\|\boldsymbol{f}_{1}\|_{\infty}\|\boldsymbol{f}_{2}\|\sum_{i=1}^{m}\left|\left(\frac{\mathbf{x}_{t-1}}{n}-\boldsymbol{\nu}_{t-1}\right)^{\top}\boldsymbol{e}_{i}\right|.

(B.59)

By the Minkowski inequality and lemma
9
there exist constants
\bar{b}_{t}>0
and
\bar{\gamma}_{t_{z}}>0
such that:

\mathbb{E}\left[\left|\boldsymbol{f}_{1}^{\top}\left[\left(\frac{\mathbf{x}_{t-1}}{n}\otimes\mathbf{1}_{m}\right)\odot\left(\mathbf{K}_{t,\boldsymbol{\eta}(\mathbf{x}_{t-1})}-\mathbf{K}_{t,\boldsymbol{\eta}(\boldsymbol{\nu}_{t-1})}\right)\right]\boldsymbol{f}_{2}\right|^{4}\right]^{\frac{1}{4}}\leq\bar{b}_{t}n^{-(\frac{1}{4}+\bar{\gamma}_{t_{z}})}

(B.60)

Moreover, (
B.54
) is equal to:

\displaystyle\left|\frac{\mathbf{x}_{t-1}^{\top}}{n}\left[\boldsymbol{f}_{1}\otimes\mathbf{1}_{m}\right]\odot\mathbf{K}_{t,\boldsymbol{\eta}(\boldsymbol{\nu}_{t-1})}\boldsymbol{f}_{2}-\boldsymbol{\nu}_{t-1}^{\top}\left[\boldsymbol{f}_{1}\otimes\mathbf{1}_{m}\right]\odot\mathbf{K}_{t,\boldsymbol{\eta}(\boldsymbol{\nu}_{t-1})}\boldsymbol{f}_{2}\right|,

(B.61)

therefore we can invoke lemma
9
with test vector
\left[\boldsymbol{f}_{1}\otimes\mathbf{1}_{m}\right]\odot\mathbf{K}_{t,\boldsymbol{\eta}(\boldsymbol{\nu}_{t-1})}\boldsymbol{f}_{2}
, this tells us there exists constants
\hat{b}_{t}>0
and
\hat{\gamma}_{t_{z}}>0
such that:

\mathbb{E}\left[\left|\frac{\mathbf{x}_{t-1}^{\top}}{n}\left[\boldsymbol{f}_{1}\otimes\mathbf{1}_{m}\right]\odot\mathbf{K}_{t,\boldsymbol{\eta}(\boldsymbol{\nu}_{t-1})}\boldsymbol{f}_{2}-\boldsymbol{\nu}_{t-1}^{\top}\left[\boldsymbol{f}_{1}\otimes\mathbf{1}_{m}\right]\odot\mathbf{K}_{t,\boldsymbol{\eta}(\boldsymbol{\nu}_{t-1})}\boldsymbol{f}_{2}\right|^{4}\right]^{\frac{1}{4}}\leq\hat{b}_{t}n^{-(\frac{1}{4}+\hat{\gamma}_{t_{z}})}.

(B.62)

We now recall that
\mathbf{Z}^{(j,k)}_{t}=\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{I}\{\xi_{t-1}^{(i)}=j,\xi_{t}^{(i)}=k\}
, so that the term (
B.52
) is equal to:

\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\underbrace{\left[\sum_{j=1}^{m}\sum_{k=1}^{m}\left(\mathbb{I}\{\xi_{t-1}^{(i)}=j,\xi_{t}^{(i)}=k\}-\mathbb{I}\{\xi_{t-1}^{(i)}=j\}K^{(j,k)}_{t,\boldsymbol{\eta}(\mathbf{x}_{t-1})}\right)f_{1}^{(j)}f_{2}^{(k)}\right]}_{=:\Delta_{t}^{(i)}}.

(B.63)

Since, conditioned on
\mathcal{G}_{t-1}\coloneqq\sigma(\{\xi_{t-1}^{(i)}\}_{i=1,\dots,n_{t}})
,
\xi_{t}^{(i)}
is a draw from the
\xi_{t-1}^{(i)}
th row of
\mathbf{K}_{t,\boldsymbol{\eta}(\mathbf{x}_{t-1})}
, we have
{\mathbb{E}\left[\Delta_{t}^{(i)}\mid\mathcal{G}_{t-1}\right]=0}
. Furthermore, given
\mathcal{G}_{t-1}
the
\Delta_{t}^{(i)}
are independent and
\left|\Delta_{t}^{(i)}\right|\leq m^{2}\|\boldsymbol{f}\|_{\infty}^{2}
. An application of lemma
7
yields that for some constants
\tilde{b}_{t}>0
and
\tilde{\gamma}_{t_{z}}>0
:

\mathbb{E}\left[\left|n^{-1}\boldsymbol{f}_{1}^{\top}\mathbf{Z}_{t}\boldsymbol{f}_{2}-\boldsymbol{f}_{1}^{\top}\left[\frac{\mathbf{x}_{t-1}}{n}\otimes\mathbf{1}_{m}\right]\odot\mathbf{K}_{t,\boldsymbol{\eta}(\mathbf{x}_{t-1})}\boldsymbol{f}_{2}\right|^{4}\right]^{\frac{1}{4}}\leq\tilde{b}_{t}n^{-(\frac{1}{4}+\tilde{\gamma}_{t_{z}})}.

(B.64)

Finally, use of the Minkowski inequality yields the result:

\mathbb{E}\left[\left|n^{-1}\boldsymbol{f}_{1}^{\top}\mathbf{Z}_{t}\boldsymbol{f}_{2}-\boldsymbol{f}_{1}^{\top}\left[\boldsymbol{\nu}_{t-1}\otimes\mathbf{1}_{m}\right]\odot\mathbf{K}_{t,\boldsymbol{\eta}(\boldsymbol{\nu}_{t})}\boldsymbol{f}_{2}\right|^{4}\right]^{\frac{1}{4}}\leq{b}_{t}n^{-(\frac{1}{4}+{\gamma}_{t_{z}})},

(B.65)

where
b_{t}=\bar{b}_{t}+\hat{b}_{t}+\tilde{b}_{t}
and
\gamma_{t_{z}}=\min(\bar{\gamma}_{t_{z}},\hat{\gamma}_{t_{z}},\tilde{\gamma}_{t_{z}})
.

∎

Lemma 12
.

Let assumptions
2
-
4
hold. For all
t\geq 1
there exists a
\bar{\gamma}_{Y}>0
, and for all vectors
\boldsymbol{f}_{1},\boldsymbol{f}_{2}\in\mathbb{R}^{m}
a constant
c_{Y}>0
, such that:

\mathbb{E}\left[\left|n^{-1}\boldsymbol{f}_{1}^{\top}\mathbf{Y}_{t}\boldsymbol{f}_{2}-\boldsymbol{f}_{1}^{\top}\left[\mathbf{N}_{t}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*})\odot\mathbf{Q}_{t}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*})\right]\boldsymbol{f}_{2}\right|^{4}\right]^{\frac{1}{4}}\leq c_{Y}n^{-(\frac{1}{4}+\bar{\gamma}_{Y})}.

Proof.

Write

\displaystyle|n^{-1}\boldsymbol{f}_{1}^{\top}\mathbf{Y}_{t}\boldsymbol{f}_{2}

\displaystyle-\boldsymbol{f}_{1}^{\top}\left[\left(\boldsymbol{\nu}_{t-1}\otimes\mathbf{1}_{m}\right)\odot\mathbf{K}_{t,\boldsymbol{\nu}_{t-1}}\odot\mathbf{Q}_{t}\right]\boldsymbol{f}_{2}|

(B.66)

\displaystyle\leq\left|n^{-1}\boldsymbol{f}_{1}^{\top}\mathbf{Y}_{t}\boldsymbol{f}_{2}-n^{-1}\boldsymbol{f}_{1}^{\top}\mathbf{Z}_{t}\odot\mathbf{Q}_{t}\boldsymbol{f}_{2}\right|

(B.67)

\displaystyle+\left|n^{-1}\boldsymbol{f}_{1}^{\top}\mathbf{Z}_{t}\odot\mathbf{Q}_{t}\boldsymbol{f}_{2}-\boldsymbol{f}_{1}^{\top}\left[\left(\boldsymbol{\nu}_{t-1}\otimes\mathbf{1}_{m}\right)\odot\mathbf{K}_{t,\boldsymbol{\nu}_{t-1}}\odot\mathbf{Q}_{t}\right]\boldsymbol{f}_{2}\right|.

(B.68)

By lemma
11
there exists
a_{Y}>0
and
\gamma_{Y_{1}}>0
such that:

\mathbb{E}\left[\left|n^{-1}\boldsymbol{f}_{1}^{\top}\mathbf{Z}_{t}\odot\mathbf{Q}_{t}\boldsymbol{f}_{2}-\boldsymbol{f}_{1}^{\top}\left[\left(\boldsymbol{\nu}_{t-1}\otimes\mathbf{1}_{m}\right)\odot\mathbf{K}_{t,\boldsymbol{\nu}_{t-1}}\odot\mathbf{Q}_{t}\right]\boldsymbol{f}_{2}\right|^{4}\right]^{\frac{1}{4}}\leq a_{Y}n^{-(\frac{1}{4}+\gamma_{Y_{1}})}

(B.69)

Now, we can write (
B.68
) as:

n^{-1}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\underbrace{\sum_{j=1}^{m}\sum_{k=1}^{m}\left[\mathbb{I}\{\xi_{t-1}^{(i)}=j,\xi_{t}^{(i)}=k\}\mathbb{I}\{\zeta^{(i)}=1\}-\mathbb{I}\{\xi_{t-1}^{(i)}=j,\xi_{t}^{(i)}=k\}Q^{(j,k)}\right]f_{1}^{(j)}f_{2}^{(k)}}_{=:\Xi_{t}^{(i)}}.

Where
\zeta^{(i)}
given
\mathcal{G}_{t-1}\vee\mathcal{G}_{t}
(where
\mathcal{G}_{t}
is defined as in lemma
9
) is distributed
\text{Bernoulli}(Q^{(\xi_{t-1}^{(i)},\xi_{t}^{(i)})})
. Hence,
{\mathbb{E}\left[\Xi^{(i)}_{t}\mid\mathcal{G}_{t-1}\vee\mathcal{G}_{t}\right]=0}
. Furthermore, given
\mathcal{G}_{t-1}\vee\mathcal{G}_{t}
the
\Xi_{t}^{(i)}
are independent and
\left|\Xi_{t}^{(i)}\right|<m^{2}\|\boldsymbol{f}\|_{\infty}^{2}
. An application of lemma
7
yields:

\mathbb{E}\left[\left|n^{-1}\boldsymbol{f}_{1}^{\top}\mathbf{Y}_{t}\boldsymbol{f}_{2}-n^{-1}\boldsymbol{f}_{1}^{\top}\mathbf{Z}_{t}\odot\mathbf{Q}_{t}\boldsymbol{f}_{2}\right|^{4}\right]^{\frac{1}{4}}\leq b_{Y}n^{-(\frac{1}{4}+\gamma_{Y_{2}})},

(B.70)

for some
b_{y}>0
and
\gamma_{Y_{2}}>0
. Use of the Minkowski inequality yields the result:

\mathbb{E}\left[\left|n^{-1}\boldsymbol{f}_{1}^{\top}\mathbf{Y}_{t}\boldsymbol{f}_{2}-\boldsymbol{f}_{1}^{\top}\left[\left(\boldsymbol{\nu}_{t-1}\otimes\mathbf{1}_{m}\right)\odot\mathbf{K}_{t,\boldsymbol{\nu}_{t-1}}\odot\mathbf{Q}_{t}\right]\boldsymbol{f}_{2}\right|^{4}\right]^{\frac{1}{4}}\leq(a_{Y}+b_{Y})n^{-(\frac{1}{4}+\bar{\gamma}_{Y})},

(B.71)

for
\bar{\gamma}_{Y}=\min(\gamma_{Y_{1}},\gamma_{Y_{2}}).

∎

Proposition 2
.

Let assumptions
2
-
4
hold. Then for all
t\geq 1
:

n^{-1}\mathbf{Y}_{t}\underset{a.s.}{\overset{\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}}{\longrightarrow}}\mathbf{N}_{t}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*})\odot\mathbf{Q}_{t}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}),

and for all
r\geq 1
,

n^{-1}\bar{\mathbf{Y}}_{r}\underset{a.s.}{\overset{\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}}{\longrightarrow}}\sum_{t=\tau_{r-1}+1}^{\tau_{r}}\mathbf{N}_{t}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*})\odot\mathbf{Q}_{t}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}).

(B.72)

Proof.

We have that by lemma
12
for all
t
there exists
c>0
and
\gamma>0
such that:

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}^{\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}}(\big|n^{-1}\boldsymbol{f}_{1}^{\top}\mathbf{Y}_{t}\boldsymbol{f}_{2}

\displaystyle-\boldsymbol{f}_{1}^{\top}\left[\left(\boldsymbol{\nu}_{t-1}\otimes\mathbf{1}_{m}\right)\odot\mathbf{K}_{t,\boldsymbol{\nu}_{t-1}}\odot\mathbf{Q}_{t}\right]\boldsymbol{f}_{2}\big|>\varepsilon)

\displaystyle\leq\varepsilon^{-4}\mathbb{E}\left[\left|n^{-1}\boldsymbol{f}_{1}^{\top}\mathbf{Y}_{t}\boldsymbol{f}_{2}-n^{-1}\boldsymbol{f}_{1}^{\top}\mathbf{Z}_{t}\odot\mathbf{Q}_{t}\boldsymbol{f}_{2}\right|^{4}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\varepsilon^{-4}cn^{-(1+\gamma)}.

It follows that:

\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}\mathbb{P}^{\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}}\left(\left|n^{-1}\boldsymbol{f}_{1}^{\top}\mathbf{Y}_{t}\boldsymbol{f}_{2}-\boldsymbol{f}_{1}^{\top}\left[\left(\boldsymbol{\nu}_{t-1}\otimes\mathbf{1}_{m}\right)\odot\mathbf{K}_{t,\boldsymbol{\nu}_{t-1}}\odot\mathbf{Q}_{t}\right]\boldsymbol{f}_{2}\right|>\varepsilon\right)<\infty.

This result along with a Borel-Cantelli argument, as in proposition
1
, completes the proof of the first claim of the proposition. The second claim follows from the first since
\bar{\mathbf{Y}}_{r}=\sum_{t=\tau_{r-1}+1}^{\tau_{r}}\mathbf{Y}_{t}
.
∎

B.2
Filtering intensity limits

B.2.1
Case (I)

Define the vectors, or
t\geq 1
:

\displaystyle\bar{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}_{0,\infty}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*},\boldsymbol{\theta})

\displaystyle\coloneqq\boldsymbol{\lambda}_{0,\infty}(\boldsymbol{\theta}),

(B.73)

\displaystyle\boldsymbol{\lambda}_{t,\infty}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*},\boldsymbol{\theta})

\displaystyle\coloneqq\left[(\bar{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}_{t-1,\infty}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*},\boldsymbol{\theta})\odot\boldsymbol{\delta}_{t}(\boldsymbol{\theta}))^{\top}\mathbf{K}_{t,\boldsymbol{\eta}(\bar{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}_{t-1,\infty}\left(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*},\boldsymbol{\theta})\odot\boldsymbol{\delta}_{t}(\boldsymbol{\theta})\right)}(\boldsymbol{\theta})\right]^{\top}+\boldsymbol{\alpha}_{t,\infty}(\boldsymbol{\theta}),

\displaystyle\boldsymbol{\mu}_{t,\infty}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*},\boldsymbol{\theta})

\displaystyle\coloneqq\left[\left(\boldsymbol{\lambda}_{t,\infty}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*},\boldsymbol{\theta})\odot\mathbf{q}_{t}(\boldsymbol{\theta})\right)^{\top}\mathbf{G}_{t}(\boldsymbol{\theta})\right]^{\top}+\boldsymbol{\kappa}_{t,\infty}(\boldsymbol{\theta}),

\displaystyle\bar{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}_{t,\infty}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*},\boldsymbol{\theta})

\displaystyle\coloneqq\bigg[\mathbf{1}_{m}-\mathbf{q}_{t}(\boldsymbol{\theta})

\displaystyle+\left(\left[\boldsymbol{\mu}_{t,\infty}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*},\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*})\oslash\boldsymbol{\mu}_{t,\infty}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*},\boldsymbol{\theta})\right]^{\top}\left(\left[\mathbf{1}_{m}\otimes\mathbf{q}_{t}(\boldsymbol{\theta})\right]\odot\mathbf{G}_{t}(\boldsymbol{\theta})^{\top}\right)\right)^{\top}\bigg]\odot\boldsymbol{\lambda}_{t,\infty}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*},\boldsymbol{\theta}),

(B.74)

where by convention, if we encounter
0/0
in the element-wise division operation we replace that ratio by
0
.

Our main objective in section
B.2.1
is to show these vectors are the
\mathbb{P}^{\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}}
-a.s. limits of the corresponding finite-
n
quantities evaluated at
\boldsymbol{\theta}
, computed using algorithm
1
. This is the subject of proposition
3
.

Proposition 3
.

Let assumptions
2
-
4
hold. Then for all
\boldsymbol{\theta}\in\Theta
and
t\geq 1
:

\displaystyle n^{-1}\boldsymbol{\mu}_{t,n}(\boldsymbol{\theta})

\displaystyle\underset{a.s.}{\overset{\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}}{\longrightarrow}}\boldsymbol{\mu}_{t,\infty}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*},\boldsymbol{\theta}),

\displaystyle n^{-1}\boldsymbol{\lambda}_{t,n}(\boldsymbol{\theta})

\displaystyle\underset{a.s.}{\overset{\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}}{\longrightarrow}}\boldsymbol{\lambda}_{t,\infty}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*},\boldsymbol{\theta}),

\displaystyle n^{-1}\bar{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}_{t,n}(\boldsymbol{\theta})

\displaystyle\underset{a.s.}{\overset{\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}}{\longrightarrow}}\bar{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}_{t,\infty}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*},\boldsymbol{\theta}).

The proof is postponed until later in section
B.2.1
.

Remark 1
.

By writing out the above definitions it can be checked that
\boldsymbol{\nu}_{t}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*})=\boldsymbol{\lambda}_{t,\infty}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*},\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*})
, hence lemma
9
implies by a Borel-Cantelli argument
n^{-1}\mathbf{x}_{t}\underset{a.s.}{\overset{\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}}{\longrightarrow}}\boldsymbol{\lambda}_{t,\infty}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*},\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*})
; and that
\boldsymbol{\mu}_{t,\infty}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*},\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*})
is equal to the right hand side of (
B.49
) in proposition
1
, hence
n^{-1}\mathbf{y}_{t}\underset{a.s.}{\overset{\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}}{\longrightarrow}}\boldsymbol{\mu}_{t,\infty}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*},\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*})
. Therefore proposition
3
implies that if algorithm
1
is run with the model specified by the DGP
\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}
, thus computing
\boldsymbol{\lambda}_{t,n}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*})
and
\boldsymbol{\mu}_{t,n}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*})
, that when rescaled by
n^{-1}
these vectors converge as
n\to\infty
to the same
\mathbb{P}^{\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}}
-almost sure limits as
n^{-1}\mathbf{x}_{t}
and
n^{-1}\mathbf{y}_{t}
. We provide empirical evidence for this remark in section
C.2
.

As preliminaries to the proof of proposition
3
we need to verify that certain quantities in algorithm
1
and the vectors defined at the start of section
B.2.1
are
\mathbb{P}^{\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}}
-a.s. well-defined and finite. This is the purpose of lemma
13
and lemma
14
. In algorithm
1
, if
\boldsymbol{\mu}^{(i)}_{t,n}(\boldsymbol{\theta})=0
and
y_{t}^{(i)}>0
, then line 3 would entail dividing a finite number by zero. Lemma
13
establishes that this happens with probability zero.

Lemma 13
.

Let assumptions
2
-
4
hold. For any
\boldsymbol{\theta}\in\Theta
,
n\in\mathbb{N}
,
i\in[m]
, and
t\geq 1
,

\mathbb{P}_{n}^{\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}}\left(\mu_{t,n}^{(i)}(\boldsymbol{\theta})=0\right)>0\implies y_{t}^{(i)}=0,\quad\mathbb{P}_{n}^{\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}}\text{-}a.s.

Proof.

Fix arbitrary
\boldsymbol{\theta}\in\Theta
and
n\in\mathbb{N}
. All a.s. statements in the proof are with respect to
\mathbb{P}^{\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}}_{n}
. We will show that for all
j\in[m]
and
t\geq 1
, the following two implications hold:

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{n}^{\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}}\left(\lambda_{t,n}^{(j)}(\boldsymbol{\theta})=0\right)>0

\displaystyle\implies x_{t}^{(j)}=0,\quad a.s.,

(B.75)

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{n}^{\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}}\left(\mu_{t,n}^{(i)}(\boldsymbol{\theta})=0\right)>0

\displaystyle\implies y_{t}^{(i)}=0,\quad a.s.

(B.76)

The proof is inductive in
t
. To initialize the induction at
t=1
, let
j\in[m]
and suppose that
\mathbb{P}_{n}^{\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}}(\lambda^{(j)}_{1,n}(\boldsymbol{\theta})=0)>0
, i.e.,

\mathbb{P}_{n}^{\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}}\left(\sum_{k=1}^{m}\bar{\lambda}^{(k)}_{0,n}(\boldsymbol{\theta})\delta_{1}^{(k)}(\boldsymbol{\theta})K^{(k,j)}_{1,\boldsymbol{\eta}(\bar{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}_{0,n}(\boldsymbol{\theta})\odot\boldsymbol{\delta}_{1}(\boldsymbol{\theta}))}(\boldsymbol{\theta})+\alpha_{1,n}^{(j)}(\boldsymbol{\theta})=0\right)>0,

then
\alpha_{1,n}^{(j)}(\boldsymbol{\theta})=0
which by assumption
2
implies
\alpha_{1,n}^{(j)}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*})=0
and hence
\hat{x}^{(j)}_{1}=0
, a.s. Furthermore, for all
k\in[m]
we must have that either:

•

\mathbb{P}_{n}^{\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}}\left(\lambda^{(k)}_{0,n}(\boldsymbol{\theta})=0\right)>0
, which, since
\lambda^{(k)}_{0,n}(\boldsymbol{\theta})
is a deterministic quantity, implies
\lambda^{(k)}_{0,n}(\boldsymbol{\theta})=0
, in turn by assumption
4
this implies
\lambda^{(k)}_{0,n}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*})=0
so that
x_{0}^{(k)}=0
a.s. and
\bar{x}_{0}^{(k)}=0
a.s.; or

•

\delta_{1}^{(k)}(\boldsymbol{\theta})=0
, which by assumption
2
implies
\delta_{1}^{(k)}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*})=0
which means
\bar{x}_{0}^{(k)}=0
a.s.; or

•

K^{(k,j)}_{1,\boldsymbol{\eta}(\bar{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}_{0,n}(\boldsymbol{\theta})\odot\boldsymbol{\delta}_{1}(\boldsymbol{\theta}))}(\boldsymbol{\theta})=0
, which by assumptions
2
,
3
, and
4
implies
K^{(k,j)}_{1,\boldsymbol{\eta}(\bar{\mathbf{x}}_{0})}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*})=0
a.s.

Hence we have for all
k\in[m]
either
\bar{x}_{0}^{(k)}=0
a.s. or
K^{(k,j)}_{1,\boldsymbol{\eta}(\bar{\mathbf{x}}_{0})}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*})=0
a.s. Since, given
\mathbf{x}_{0}
,
\tilde{x}_{1}^{(j)}\sim\sum_{k=1}^{m}\text{Bin}\left(x_{0}^{(k)},K^{(k,j)}_{1,\boldsymbol{\eta}(\bar{\mathbf{x}}_{0})}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*})\right)
we must have that
\tilde{x}_{1}^{(j)}=0
a.s., therefore we have that
x_{1}^{(j)}=\tilde{x}_{1}^{(j)}+\hat{x}_{1}^{(j)}=0
a.s. We have thus proved (
B.75
) in the case
t=1
.

Now let us prove (
B.76
) in the case
t=1
. Suppose that for some
i\in[m]
,
\mathbb{P}_{n}^{\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}}\left(\mu^{(i)}_{1,n}(\boldsymbol{\theta})=0\right)>0
, i.e.,

\mathbb{P}_{n}^{\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}}\left(\sum_{j=1}^{m}\lambda^{(j)}_{1,n}(\boldsymbol{\theta})q_{1}^{(j)}(\boldsymbol{\theta})G_{1}^{(j,i)}(\boldsymbol{\theta})+\kappa^{(i)}_{1,n}(\boldsymbol{\theta})=0\right)>0.

Then
\kappa_{1,n}^{(i)}(\boldsymbol{\theta})=0
which by assumption
2
implies
\kappa_{1,n}^{(i)}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*})=0
and hence
\hat{y}^{(i)}_{1}=0
a.s. Furthermore, for all
j\in[m]
we must have that either:

•

\mathbb{P}_{n}^{\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}}\left(\lambda^{(j)}_{1,n}(\boldsymbol{\theta})=0\right)>0
, which implies
x_{1}^{(j)}=0
a.s. which implies
\bar{y}_{1}^{(j)}=0
a.s.; or

•

q_{1}^{(j)}(\boldsymbol{\theta})=0
, which by assumption
2
implies that
q_{1}^{(j)}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*})=0\implies\bar{y}_{1}^{(j)}=0
a.s.; or

•

G_{1}^{(j,i)}(\boldsymbol{\theta})=0
, which by assumption
2
implies
G_{1}^{(j,i)}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*})=0
.

Given,
\mathbf{\bar{y}}_{1}
,
\tilde{y}_{1}^{(i)}\sim\sum_{j=1}^{m}\text{Bin}\left(\bar{y}_{1}^{(j)},G_{1}^{(j,i)}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*})\right)
. This means that
\tilde{y}_{1}^{(i)}=0
a.s., and furthermore that
y_{1}^{(i)}=\tilde{y}_{1}^{(i)}+\hat{y}_{1}^{(i)}=0
a.s. This completes the proof of (
B.76
) in the case
t=1
.

As an induction hypothesis suppose that (
B.75
) and (
B.76
) hold at
t
. We shall show that
\mathbb{P}_{n}^{\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}}\left(\lambda^{(j)}_{t+1,n}(\boldsymbol{\theta})=0\right)>0\implies x_{t+1}^{(j)}=0
a.s. Firstly we will show that, for all
k\in[m]
,
\mathbb{P}_{n}^{\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}}\left(\bar{\lambda}^{(k)}_{t,n}(\boldsymbol{\theta})=0\right)>0\implies\mathbb{P}_{n}^{\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}}\left(\lambda^{(k)}_{t,n}(\boldsymbol{\theta})=0\right)>0
which, by the induction hypothesis, would imply
x^{(k)}_{t}=0
a.s. Suppose that for some
k\in[m]
,
\mathbb{P}_{n}^{\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}}\left(\bar{\lambda}^{(k)}_{t,n}(\boldsymbol{\theta})=0\right)>0
, i.e.,

\mathbb{P}_{n}^{\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}}\left((1-q_{t}^{(k)}(\boldsymbol{\theta}))\lambda^{(k)}_{t,n}(\boldsymbol{\theta})+\sum_{j=1}^{m}y_{t}^{(j)}\frac{\lambda^{(k)}_{t,n}(\boldsymbol{\theta})q_{t}^{(k)}(\boldsymbol{\theta})G_{t}^{(k,j)}(\boldsymbol{\theta})}{\mu^{(j)}_{t,n}(\boldsymbol{\theta})}=0\right)>0.

Firstly,
\bar{\lambda}^{(k)}_{t,n}(\boldsymbol{\theta})
is almost surely well defined by the induction hypothesis, since the event
\mu^{(j)}_{t,n}(\boldsymbol{\theta})=0
and
y_{t}^{(j)}>0
has probability
0
for each
j\in[m]
. Now if the above displayed inequality holds we must have that either:

•

q_{t}^{(k)}(\boldsymbol{\theta})<1
, in which case we must have
\mathbb{P}_{n}^{\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}}\left(\lambda^{(k)}_{t,n}(\boldsymbol{\theta})=0\right)>0
; or

•

q_{t}^{(k)}(\boldsymbol{\theta})=1
, in which case we must have
\mathbb{P}_{n}^{\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}}\left(\lambda^{(k)}_{t,n}(\boldsymbol{\theta})G_{t}^{(k,j)}(\boldsymbol{\theta})=0\right)>0
for all
j
so that the sum is equal to
0
with positive probability, and since
\mathbf{G}_{t}
is row-stochastic matrix, there must exist a
j\in[m]
such that
G_{t}^{(k,j)}(\boldsymbol{\theta})>0
, hence
\mathbb{P}_{n}^{\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}}\left(\lambda^{(k)}_{t,n}(\boldsymbol{\theta})=0\right)>0
.

We have thus shown
\mathbb{P}_{n}^{\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}}\left(\bar{\lambda}^{(k)}_{t,n}(\boldsymbol{\theta})=0\right)>0\implies\mathbb{P}_{n}^{\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}}\left(\lambda^{(k)}_{t,n}(\boldsymbol{\theta})=0\right)>0
which by the induction hypothesis implies
x_{t}^{(k)}=0
a.s. so that further
\bar{x}_{t}^{(k)}=0
a.s. Now if for some
j\in[m]
,
\mathbb{P}_{n}^{\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}}\left(\lambda^{(j)}_{t+1,n}(\boldsymbol{\theta})=0\right)>0
, i.e.,

\mathbb{P}_{n}^{\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}}\left(\sum_{k=1}^{m}\bar{\lambda}^{(k)}_{t,n}(\boldsymbol{\theta})\delta_{t+1}^{(k)}(\boldsymbol{\theta})K^{(k,j)}_{t+1,\boldsymbol{\eta}(\bar{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}_{t,n}(\boldsymbol{\theta})\odot\boldsymbol{\delta}_{t+1}(\boldsymbol{\theta}))}(\boldsymbol{\theta})+\alpha_{t+1,n}^{(j)}(\boldsymbol{\theta})=0\right)>0,

then
\alpha_{t+1,n}^{(j)}(\boldsymbol{\theta})=0
, which by assumption
2
implies
\alpha_{t+1,n}^{(j)}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*})=0
, hence
\hat{x}_{t}^{(j)}=0
a.s. Furthermore, for all
k\in[m]
we must have that either:

•

\mathbb{P}_{n}^{\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}}\left(\bar{\lambda}^{(k)}_{t,n}(\boldsymbol{\theta})=0\right)>0
, which implies
x_{t}^{(k)}=0
a.s.
\implies\bar{x}_{t}^{(k)}=0
a.s.; or

•

\delta_{t+1}^{(k)}(\boldsymbol{\theta})=0
, which by assumption
2
implies
\delta_{t+1}^{(k)}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*})=0\implies\bar{x}_{t}^{(k)}=0
a.s.; or

•

\mathbb{P}_{n}^{\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}}\left(K^{(k,j)}_{t+1,\boldsymbol{\eta}(\bar{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}_{t,n}(\boldsymbol{\theta})\odot\boldsymbol{\delta}_{t+1}(\boldsymbol{\theta}))}(\boldsymbol{\theta})=0\right)>0
. We claim this implies that
K^{(k,j)}_{t+1,\boldsymbol{\eta}(\bar{\mathbf{x}}_{t})}(\boldsymbol{\theta})=0
, a.s. Suppose, for contradiction, that
\mathbb{P}_{n}^{\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}}\left(K^{(k,j)}_{t+1,\boldsymbol{\eta}(\bar{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}_{t,n}(\boldsymbol{\theta})\odot\boldsymbol{\delta}_{t+1}(\boldsymbol{\theta}))}(\boldsymbol{\theta})=0\right)>0
and
\mathbb{P}_{n}^{\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}}\left(K^{(k,j)}_{t+1,\boldsymbol{\eta}(\bar{\mathbf{x}}_{t})}(\boldsymbol{\theta})>0\right)>0
. Then there exist
E,E^{\prime}\subseteq\Omega_{n}
with
\mathbb{P}_{n}^{\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}}(E)>0
and
\mathbb{P}_{n}^{\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}}(E^{\prime})>0
such that for all
\omega\in E
and all
\omega^{\prime}\in E^{\prime}
:

K^{(k,j)}_{t+1,\boldsymbol{\eta}(\bar{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}_{t,n}(\boldsymbol{\theta},\omega)\odot\boldsymbol{\delta}_{t+1}(\boldsymbol{\theta}))}(\boldsymbol{\theta})=0\text{ and }K^{(k,j)}_{t+1,\boldsymbol{\eta}(\bar{\mathbf{x}}_{t}(\omega^{\prime}))}(\boldsymbol{\theta})>0,

which implies:

\text{supp}\left(\mathbf{K}^{(k,\cdot)}_{t+1,\boldsymbol{\eta}(\bar{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}_{t,n}(\boldsymbol{\theta},\omega)\odot\boldsymbol{\delta}_{t+1}(\boldsymbol{\theta}))}(\boldsymbol{\theta})\right)\not\subseteq\text{supp}\left(\mathbf{K}^{(k,\cdot)}_{t+1,\boldsymbol{\eta}(\bar{\mathbf{x}}_{t}(\omega^{\prime}))}(\boldsymbol{\theta})\right).

By assumption
3
this implies:

\text{supp}\left(\bar{\mathbf{x}}_{t}(\omega^{\prime})\right)\not\subseteq\text{supp}\left(\bar{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}_{t,n}(\boldsymbol{\theta},\omega)\odot\boldsymbol{\delta}_{t+1}(\boldsymbol{\theta})\right),

i.e. there exists
l
such that:

\left(\bar{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}_{t,n}(\boldsymbol{\theta},\omega)\odot\boldsymbol{\delta}_{t+1}(\boldsymbol{\theta})\right)^{(l)}=0\text{ and }\left(\bar{\mathbf{x}}_{t}(\omega^{\prime})\right)^{(l)}>0.

But since
\mathbb{P}_{n}^{\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}}(E)>0
and
\mathbb{P}_{n}^{\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}}(E^{\prime})>0
this implies that:

\mathbb{P}_{n}^{\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}}(\bar{\lambda}^{(l)}_{t,n}(\boldsymbol{\theta})=0)>0\text{ and }\mathbb{P}_{n}^{\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}}(\bar{x}^{(l)}_{t}>0)>0.

This contradicts the observation in the first bullet point, hence
K^{(k,j)}_{t+1,\boldsymbol{\eta}(\bar{\mathbf{x}}_{t})}(\boldsymbol{\theta})=0
a.s. Then by assumption
2
we have
{K^{(k,j)}_{t+1,\boldsymbol{\eta}(\bar{\mathbf{x}}_{t})}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*})=0}
a.s.

Hence, similarly to the argument used in the case
t=1
, we must have that
\tilde{x}_{t+1}^{(j)}=0
a.s. so that
x_{t+1}^{(j)}=\tilde{x}_{t+1}^{(j)}+\hat{x}_{t+1}^{(j)}=0
a.s. Thus
(
B.75
) holds with
t
replace by
t+1
.

It remains to show that (
B.76
) holds with
t
replaced by
t+1
. So suppose that for some
i\in[m]
,
\mathbb{P}_{n}^{\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}}\left(\mu^{(i)}_{t+1,n}(\boldsymbol{\theta})=0\right)>0
, i.e.,

\mathbb{P}_{n}^{\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}}\left(\sum_{j=1}^{m}\lambda^{(j)}_{t+1,n}(\boldsymbol{\theta})q_{t+1}^{(j)}(\boldsymbol{\theta})G_{t+1}^{(j,i)}(\boldsymbol{\theta})+\kappa^{(i)}_{t+1,n}(\boldsymbol{\theta})=0\right)>0,

then we must have
\kappa^{(i)}_{t+1,n}(\boldsymbol{\theta})=0
which by assumption
2
implies
\kappa^{(i)}_{t+1,n}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*})=0
hence
\hat{y}^{(i)}_{t+1}=0
a.s. Furthermore, for all
j\in[m]
we must have either:

•

\mathbb{P}_{n}^{\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}}\left(\lambda^{(j)}_{t+1,n}(\boldsymbol{\theta})=0\right)>0
, which implies that

x^{(j)}_{t+1}=0\implies\bar{y}_{t+1}^{(j)}=0
a.s.; or

•

q_{t+1}^{(j)}(\boldsymbol{\theta})=0
, which by assumption
2
implies
q_{t+1}^{(j)}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*})=0\implies\bar{y}_{t+1}^{(j)}=0
a.s.; or

•

G_{t+1}^{(j,i)}(\boldsymbol{\theta})=0
, which by assumption
2
implies
G_{t+1}^{(j,i)}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*})=0
.

Hence, using the same reasoning as in the
t=1
case, we have
\tilde{y}_{t+1}^{(i)}=0
a.s. and furthermore
{y}_{t+1}^{(i)}=\tilde{y}_{t+1}^{(i)}+\hat{y}_{t+1}^{(i)}=0
a.s. This completes the proof of (
B.76
) with
t
replaced by
t+1
. The induction is therefore complete.
∎

If
\mu^{(i)}_{t,\infty}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*},\boldsymbol{\theta})=0
and
\mu^{(i)}_{t,\infty}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*},\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*})>0
then
\bar{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}_{t,\infty}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*},\boldsymbol{\theta})
would involve division of a finite number by zero. Lemma
14
establishes that this situation cannot arise.

Lemma 14
.

Let assumptions
2
-
4
hold. For any
\boldsymbol{\theta},\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\prime}\in\Theta
,
i\in[m]
and
t\geq 1
,

\displaystyle\bar{\lambda}^{(j)}_{t,\infty}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*},\boldsymbol{\theta})=0

\displaystyle\implies\bar{\lambda}^{(j)}_{t,\infty}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*},\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\prime})=0,

(B.77)

\displaystyle\mu^{(i)}_{t,\infty}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*},\boldsymbol{\theta})=0

\displaystyle\implies\mu^{(i)}_{t,\infty}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*},\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\prime})=0.

(B.78)

Proof.

Fix arbitrary
\boldsymbol{\theta},\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\prime}\in\Theta
. By symmetry we only need to prove the implication in one direction. We will show that the following two implications hold for all
i,j\in[m]
and
t\geq 1
:

\displaystyle\lambda^{(j)}_{t,\infty}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*},\boldsymbol{\theta})=0

\displaystyle\implies\lambda^{(j)}_{t,\infty}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*},\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\prime})=0,

(B.79)

\displaystyle\mu^{(i)}_{t,\infty}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*},\boldsymbol{\theta})=0

\displaystyle\implies\mu^{(i)}_{t,\infty}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*},\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\prime})=0.

(B.80)

For the
t=1
case, if
\lambda^{(j)}_{1,\infty}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*},\boldsymbol{\theta})=0
, i.e.,

\sum_{k=1}^{m}\lambda^{(k)}_{0,\infty}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*},\boldsymbol{\theta})\delta_{1}^{(k)}(\boldsymbol{\theta})K^{(k,j)}_{1,\boldsymbol{\eta}(\boldsymbol{\lambda}_{0,\infty}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*},\boldsymbol{\theta})\odot\boldsymbol{\delta}(\boldsymbol{\theta}))}(\boldsymbol{\theta})+\alpha^{(j)}_{1,\infty}(\boldsymbol{\theta})=0,

then
\alpha^{(j)}_{1,\infty}(\boldsymbol{\theta})=0
which by assumption
2
implies
\alpha^{(j)}_{1,\infty}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\prime})=0
. Furthermore, for each
j\in[m]
we must have either:

•

\lambda^{(k)}_{0,\infty}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*},\boldsymbol{\theta})=\lambda^{(k)}_{0,\infty}(\boldsymbol{\theta})=0
, which by assumption
4
implies
\lambda^{(k)}_{0,\infty}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\prime})=0
; or

•

\delta_{1}^{(k)}(\boldsymbol{\theta})=0
, which by assumption
2
implies
\delta_{1}^{(k)}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\prime})=0
; or

•

K^{(k,j)}_{1,\boldsymbol{\eta}(\boldsymbol{\lambda}_{0,\infty}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*},\boldsymbol{\theta})\odot\boldsymbol{\delta}(\boldsymbol{\theta}))}(\boldsymbol{\theta})=0
, which by assumptions
2
,
3
, and
4
implies

K^{(k,j)}_{1,\boldsymbol{\eta}(\boldsymbol{\lambda}_{0,\infty}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*},\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\prime})\odot\boldsymbol{\delta}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\prime}))}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\prime})=0
.

Hence we have:

\lambda^{(j)}_{1,\infty}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*},\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\prime})=\sum_{k=1}^{m}\lambda^{(k)}_{0,\infty}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*},\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\prime})\delta_{1}^{(k)}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\prime})K^{(k,j)}_{1,\boldsymbol{\eta}(\boldsymbol{\lambda}_{0,\infty}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*},\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\prime})\odot\boldsymbol{\delta}_{1}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\prime}))}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\prime})+\alpha^{(j)}_{1,\infty}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\prime})=0,

so (
B.79
) holds with
t=1
. In order to establish (
B.80
) with
t=1
, consider

\mu^{(i)}_{1,\infty}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*},\boldsymbol{\theta})=\sum_{j=1}^{m}\lambda^{(j)}_{1,\infty}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*},\boldsymbol{\theta})q_{1}^{(j)}(\boldsymbol{\theta})G_{1}^{(i,j)}(\boldsymbol{\theta})+\kappa^{(i)}_{1,\infty}(\boldsymbol{\theta})=0,

hence
\kappa^{(i)}_{1,\infty}(\boldsymbol{\theta})=0
, which by assumption
2
implies
\kappa^{(i)}_{1,\infty}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\prime})=0
. Furthermore, for each
j\in[m]
we must have either:

•

\lambda^{(j)}_{1,\infty}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*},\boldsymbol{\theta})=0
, which by the above implies
\lambda^{(j)}_{1,\infty}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*},\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\prime})=0
; or

•

q_{1}^{(j)}(\boldsymbol{\theta})=0
, which by assumption
2
implies
q_{1}^{(j)}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\prime})=0
; or

•

G_{1}^{(i,j)}(\boldsymbol{\theta})=0
, which by assumption
2
implies
G_{1}^{(i,j)}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\prime})=0
.

Hence:

\mu^{(i)}_{1,\infty}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*},\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\prime})=\sum_{j=1}^{m}\lambda^{(j)}_{1,\infty}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*},\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\prime})q_{1}^{(j)}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\prime})G_{1}^{(i,j)}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\prime})+\kappa^{(j)}_{1,\infty}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\prime})=0.

Thus we have shown that (
B.80
) holds with
t=1
.

For the induction hypothesis, assume that (
B.79
) and (
B.80
) with hold for some
t\geq 1
. Then for each
k\in[m]
write:

\bar{\lambda}^{(k)}_{t,\infty}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*},\boldsymbol{\theta})=(1-q^{(k)}_{t}(\boldsymbol{\theta}))\lambda^{(k)}_{t,\infty}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*},\boldsymbol{\theta})+\sum_{j=1}^{m}\mu^{(i)}_{t,\infty}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*},\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*})\frac{\lambda^{(k)}_{t,\infty}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*},\boldsymbol{\theta})q^{(k)}_{t}(\boldsymbol{\theta})G^{(k,j)}_{t}(\boldsymbol{\theta})}{\mu^{(i)}_{t,\infty}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*},\boldsymbol{\theta})}=0.

This is well defined by the induction hypothesis choosing
\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\prime}=\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}
. Furthermore, we must have either:

•

q_{t}^{(k)}(\boldsymbol{\theta})<1
, in which case we must have
\lambda^{(k)}_{t,\infty}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*},\boldsymbol{\theta})=0\implies\lambda^{(k)}_{t,\infty}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*},\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\prime})=0
; or

•

q_{t}^{(k)}(\boldsymbol{\theta})=1
, in which case we must have
\lambda^{(k)}_{t,\infty}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*},\boldsymbol{\theta})G_{t}^{(k,j)}(\boldsymbol{\theta})=0
a.s. for all
j
so that the sum is equal to
0
. Since
\mathbf{G}_{t}
is row-stochastic matrix, we know there must exist a
j\in[m]
such that
G_{t}^{(k,j)}(\boldsymbol{\theta})>0
and so we must have
\lambda^{(k)}_{t,\infty}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*},\boldsymbol{\theta})=0\implies\lambda^{(k)}_{t,\infty}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*},\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\prime})=0
.

So we have
\bar{\lambda}^{(k)}_{t,\infty}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*},\boldsymbol{\theta})=0\implies\lambda^{(k)}_{t,\infty}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*},\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\prime})=0
, indeed the reverse implication is also true by definition of
\bar{\lambda}^{(k)}_{t,\infty}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*},\boldsymbol{\theta})
so that
\bar{\lambda}^{(k)}_{t,\infty}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*},\boldsymbol{\theta})=0\iff\lambda^{(k)}_{t,\infty}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*},\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\prime})=0
. Now consider

\lambda^{(j)}_{t+1,\infty}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*},\boldsymbol{\theta})=\sum_{k=1}^{m}\bar{\lambda}^{(k)}_{t,\infty}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*},\boldsymbol{\theta})\delta_{t+1}^{(k)}(\boldsymbol{\theta})K^{(k,j)}_{1,\boldsymbol{\eta}(\boldsymbol{\bar{\lambda}}_{t,\infty}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*},\boldsymbol{\theta})\odot\boldsymbol{\delta}_{t+1}(\boldsymbol{\theta}))}(\boldsymbol{\theta})+\alpha^{(j)}_{t+1,\infty}(\boldsymbol{\theta})=0,

then
\alpha^{(j)}_{t+1,\infty}(\boldsymbol{\theta})=\alpha^{(j)}_{t+1,\infty}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\prime})=0
and for all
k\in[m]
we must have either:

•

\bar{\lambda}^{(k)}_{t,\infty}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*},\boldsymbol{\theta})=0
, which implies by the above that
\lambda^{(k)}_{t,\infty}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*},\boldsymbol{\theta})=0\implies\lambda^{(k)}_{t,\infty}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*},\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\prime})=0\implies\bar{\lambda}^{(k)}_{t,\infty}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*},\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\prime})
; or

•

\delta_{t+1}^{(k)}(\boldsymbol{\theta})=0
, which by assumption
2
that
\delta_{t+1}^{(k)}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\prime})=0
; or

•

K^{(k,j)}_{1,\boldsymbol{\eta}(\boldsymbol{\bar{\lambda}}_{t,\infty}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*},\boldsymbol{\theta})\odot\boldsymbol{\delta}_{t+1}(\boldsymbol{\theta}))}(\boldsymbol{\theta})=0
which by assumptions
2
,
3
, and the induction hypothesis implies
K^{(k,j)}_{1,\boldsymbol{\eta}(\boldsymbol{\bar{\lambda}}_{t,\infty}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*},\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\prime})\odot\boldsymbol{\delta}_{t+1}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\prime}))}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\prime})=0
.

Hence

\lambda^{(j)}_{t+1,\infty}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*},\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\prime})=\sum_{k=1}^{m}\bar{\lambda}^{(k)}_{t,\infty}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*},\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\prime})\delta_{t+1}^{(k)}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\prime})K^{(k,j)}_{1,\boldsymbol{\eta}(\boldsymbol{\bar{\lambda}}_{t,\infty}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*},\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\prime})\odot\boldsymbol{\delta}_{t+1}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\prime}))}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\prime})+\alpha^{(j)}_{t+1,\infty}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\prime})=0.

Now, if for some
i\in[m]
:

\mu^{(i)}_{t+1,\infty}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*},\boldsymbol{\theta})=\sum_{j=1}^{m}\lambda^{(j)}_{t+1,\infty}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*},\boldsymbol{\theta})q_{t+1}^{(j)}(\boldsymbol{\theta})G_{t+1}^{(j,i)}(\boldsymbol{\theta})+\kappa^{(i)}_{t+1,\infty}(\boldsymbol{\theta})=0,

then
\kappa^{(i)}_{t+1,\infty}(\boldsymbol{\theta})=0
, which by assumption
2
implies
\kappa^{(i)}_{t+1,\infty}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\prime})=0
and for all
j\in[m]
we have either:

•

\lambda^{(j)}_{t+1,\infty}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*},\boldsymbol{\theta})=0
, which by the above implies
\lambda^{(j)}_{t+1,\infty}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*},\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\prime})=0
; or

•

q_{t+1}^{(j)}(\boldsymbol{\theta})=0
, which by assumption
2
implies
q_{t+1}^{(j)}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\prime})=0
; or

•

G_{t+1}^{(j,i)}(\boldsymbol{\theta})=0
, which by assumption
2
implies
G_{t+1}^{(j,i)}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\prime})=0
.

Hence

\mu^{(i)}_{t+1,\infty}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*},\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\prime})=\sum_{j=1}^{m}\lambda^{(j)}_{t+1,\infty}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*},\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\prime})q_{t+1}^{(j)}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\prime})G_{t+1}^{(i,j)}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\prime})+\kappa^{(i)}_{t+1,\infty}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\prime})=0,

(B.81)

and the inductive proof is complete.
∎

The following lemma will be used in the proof of lemma
5
.

Lemma 15
.

Let assumptions
2
-
4
hold. For all
\boldsymbol{\theta}\in\Theta
,
n\in\mathbb{N}
, and
i\in[m]
:

\displaystyle\lambda^{(j)}_{t,\infty}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*},\boldsymbol{\theta})=0

\displaystyle\implies\lambda^{(j)}_{t,n}(\boldsymbol{\theta})=0\quad a.s.,

\displaystyle\mu^{(i)}_{t,\infty}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*},\boldsymbol{\theta})=0

\displaystyle\implies\mu^{(i)}_{t,n}(\boldsymbol{\theta})=0\quad a.s..

Proof.

Fix arbitrary
\boldsymbol{\theta},\in\Theta
and
n\in\mathbb{N}
. All almost sure statements in the proof are made with respect to
\mathbb{P}_{n}^{\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}}
. We will show by induction that the following two implications hold for all
t\geq 1
and
i,j\in[m]
.

\displaystyle\lambda^{(j)}_{t,\infty}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*},\boldsymbol{\theta})=0

\displaystyle\implies\lambda^{(j)}_{t,n}(\boldsymbol{\theta})=0\quad a.s.,

(B.82)

\displaystyle\mu^{(i)}_{t,\infty}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*},\boldsymbol{\theta})=0

\displaystyle\implies\mu^{(i)}_{t,n}(\boldsymbol{\theta})=0\quad a.s.

(B.83)

For
t=1
consider:

\lambda^{(j)}_{1,\infty}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*},\boldsymbol{\theta})=\sum_{k=1}^{m}\bar{\lambda}^{(k)}_{0,\infty}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*},\boldsymbol{\theta})\delta_{1}^{(k)}(\boldsymbol{\theta})K^{(k,j)}_{1,\boldsymbol{\eta}(\boldsymbol{\lambda}_{0,\infty}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*},\boldsymbol{\theta})\odot\boldsymbol{\delta}_{1}(\boldsymbol{\theta}))}(\boldsymbol{\theta})+\alpha^{(j)}_{1,\infty}(\boldsymbol{\theta})=0,

then
\alpha^{(j)}_{1,\infty}(\boldsymbol{\theta})=0
which by assumption
2
implies
\alpha^{(j)}_{1,n}(\boldsymbol{\theta})=0
, and for all
j\in[m]
we must have either:

•

\bar{\lambda}^{(k)}_{0,\infty}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*},\boldsymbol{\theta})=\lambda^{(k)}_{0,\infty}(\boldsymbol{\theta})=0
, which by assumption
4
implies
\lambda^{(k)}_{0,n}(\boldsymbol{\theta})=0
; or

•

\delta_{1}^{(k)}(\boldsymbol{\theta})=0
; or

•

K^{(k,j)}_{1,\boldsymbol{\eta}(\boldsymbol{\lambda}_{0,\infty}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*},\boldsymbol{\theta})\odot\boldsymbol{\delta}(\boldsymbol{\theta}))}(\boldsymbol{\theta})=0
which by assumptions
3
and
4
implies
K^{(k,j)}_{1,\boldsymbol{\eta}(\boldsymbol{\lambda}_{0,n}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*},\boldsymbol{\theta})\odot\boldsymbol{\delta}(\boldsymbol{\theta}))}(\boldsymbol{\theta})=0
.

Hence we have:

\lambda^{(j)}_{1,n}(\boldsymbol{\theta})=\sum_{k=1}^{m}\lambda^{(k)}_{0,n}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\prime})\delta_{1}^{(k)}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\prime})K^{(k,j)}_{1,\boldsymbol{\eta}(\boldsymbol{\lambda}_{0,n}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\prime})\odot\boldsymbol{\delta}_{1}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\prime}))}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\prime})+\alpha^{(j)}_{1,n}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\prime})=0,\quad a.s.

Now consider:

\mu^{(i)}_{1,\infty}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*},\boldsymbol{\theta})=\sum_{j=1}^{m}\lambda^{(j)}_{1,\infty}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*},\boldsymbol{\theta})q_{1}^{(j)}(\boldsymbol{\theta})G_{1}^{(i,j)}(\boldsymbol{\theta})+\kappa^{(i)}_{1,\infty}(\boldsymbol{\theta})=0,

then
\kappa^{(i)}_{1,\infty}(\boldsymbol{\theta})=0
, which by assumption
2
implies
\kappa^{(i)}_{1,n}(\boldsymbol{\theta})=0
, furthermore for al
j\in[m]
we must have either:

•

\lambda^{(j)}_{1,\infty}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*},\boldsymbol{\theta})=0
, which by the above implies
\lambda^{(j)}_{1,n}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\prime})=0\quad\mathbb{P}^{\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}}a.s.
; or

•

q_{1}^{(j)}(\boldsymbol{\theta})=0
; or

•

G_{1}^{(i,j)}(\boldsymbol{\theta})=0
.

Hence:

\mu^{(i)}_{1,n}(\boldsymbol{\theta})=\sum_{j=1}^{m}\lambda^{(j)}_{1,n}(\boldsymbol{\theta})q_{1}^{(j)}(\boldsymbol{\theta})G_{1}^{(k,j)}(\boldsymbol{\theta})+\kappa^{(i)}_{1,n}(\boldsymbol{\theta})=0.

For the induction hypothesis, assume (
B.82
) and (
B.83
) hold. Then for each
k\in[m]
,

\bar{\lambda}^{(k)}_{t,\infty}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*},\boldsymbol{\theta})=(1-q^{(k)}_{t}(\boldsymbol{\theta}))\lambda^{(k)}_{t,\infty}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*},\boldsymbol{\theta})+\sum_{j=1}^{m}\mu^{(i)}_{t,\infty}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*},\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*})\frac{\lambda^{(k)}_{t,\infty}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*},\boldsymbol{\theta})q^{(k)}_{t}(\boldsymbol{\theta})G^{(k,j)}_{t}(\boldsymbol{\theta})}{\mu^{(i)}_{t,\infty}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*},\boldsymbol{\theta})}=0.

This is well defined by the induction hypothesis. Furthermore, in order for this equality with zero to hold we must have either:

•

q_{t}^{(k)}(\boldsymbol{\theta})<1
, in which case we must have
\lambda^{(k)}_{t,\infty}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*},\boldsymbol{\theta})=0
which by the induction hypothesis implies
\lambda^{(k)}_{t,n}(\boldsymbol{\theta})=0\quad a.s.
; or

•

q_{t}^{(k)}(\boldsymbol{\theta})=1
, in which case we must have
\lambda^{(k)}_{t,\infty}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*},\boldsymbol{\theta})G_{t}^{(k,j)}(\boldsymbol{\theta})=0\quad a.s.
for all
j
so that the sum is equal to
0
. Since
\mathbf{G}_{t}
is row-stochastic matrix, we know there must exist a
j\in[m]
such that
G_{t}^{(k,j)}(\boldsymbol{\theta})>0
and so we must have
\lambda^{(k)}_{t,\infty}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*},\boldsymbol{\theta})=0
which by the induction hypothesis implies
\lambda^{(k)}_{t,n}(\boldsymbol{\theta})=0
a.s.

So we have
\bar{\lambda}^{(k)}_{t,\infty}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*},\boldsymbol{\theta})=0\implies\lambda^{(k)}_{t,n}(\boldsymbol{\theta})=0
a.s., furthermore
\lambda^{(k)}_{t,n}(\boldsymbol{\theta})=0
a.s.
\implies

\bar{\lambda}^{(k)}_{t,n}(\boldsymbol{\theta})=0
a.s.. Now if for some
j\in[m]
:

\lambda^{(j)}_{t+1,\infty}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*},\boldsymbol{\theta})=\sum_{k=1}^{m}\bar{\lambda}^{(k)}_{t,\infty}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*},\boldsymbol{\theta})\delta_{t+1}^{(k)}(\boldsymbol{\theta})K^{(k,j)}_{1,\boldsymbol{\eta}(\boldsymbol{\bar{\lambda}}_{t,\infty}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*},\boldsymbol{\theta})\odot\boldsymbol{\delta}_{t+1}(\boldsymbol{\theta}))}(\boldsymbol{\theta})+\alpha^{(j)}_{t+1,\infty}(\boldsymbol{\theta})=0,

(B.84)

then
\alpha^{(j)}_{t+1,\infty}(\boldsymbol{\theta})=0
which by assumption
2
implies
\alpha^{(j)}_{t+1,n}(\boldsymbol{\theta})=0
and for each
k\in[m]
we must have either:

•

\bar{\lambda}^{(k)}_{t,\infty}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*},\boldsymbol{\theta})=0
, which we have already shown implies
\lambda^{(k)}_{t,n}(\boldsymbol{\theta})=0\quad a.s.\implies\bar{\lambda}^{(k)}_{t,n}(\boldsymbol{\theta})=0\quad a.s.
; or

•

\delta_{t+1}^{(k)}(\boldsymbol{\theta})=0
; or

•

K^{(k,j)}_{1,\boldsymbol{\eta}(\boldsymbol{\bar{\lambda}}_{t,\infty}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*},\boldsymbol{\theta})\odot\boldsymbol{\delta}_{t+1}(\boldsymbol{\theta}))}(\boldsymbol{\theta})=0
, which together with assumption
3
implies
K^{(k,j)}_{1,\boldsymbol{\eta}(\bar{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}_{t,n}(\boldsymbol{\theta})\odot\boldsymbol{\delta}_{t+1}(\boldsymbol{\theta}))}(\boldsymbol{\theta})=0
.

Hence

\lambda^{(j)}_{t+1,n}(\boldsymbol{\theta})=\sum_{k=1}^{m}\bar{\lambda}^{(k)}_{t,n}\boldsymbol{\theta})\delta_{t+1}^{(k)}(\boldsymbol{\theta})K^{(k,j)}_{1,\boldsymbol{\eta}(\boldsymbol{\bar{\lambda}}_{t,n}(\boldsymbol{\theta})\odot\boldsymbol{\delta}_{t+1}(\boldsymbol{\theta}))}(\boldsymbol{\theta})+\alpha^{(j)}_{t+1,n}(\boldsymbol{\theta})=0\quad a.s.

Now, if

\mu^{(i)}_{t+1,\infty}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*},\boldsymbol{\theta})=\sum_{j=1}^{m}\lambda^{(j)}_{t+1,\infty}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*},\boldsymbol{\theta})q_{t+1}^{(j)}(\boldsymbol{\theta})G_{t+1}^{(k,j)}(\boldsymbol{\theta})+\kappa^{(i)}_{t+1,\infty}(\boldsymbol{\theta})=0,

then
\kappa^{(i)}_{t+1,\infty}(\boldsymbol{\theta})=0
which by assumption
4
implies
\kappa^{(i)}_{t+1,n}(\boldsymbol{\theta})=0
and for all
j\in[m]
we have either:

•

\lambda^{(j)}_{t+1,\infty}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*},\boldsymbol{\theta})=0
, which implies
\lambda^{(j)}_{t+1,n}(\boldsymbol{\theta})=0
a.s.; or

•

q_{t+1}^{(j)}(\boldsymbol{\theta})=0
; or

•

G_{t+1}^{(k,j)}(\boldsymbol{\theta})=0
.

Hence

\mu^{(i)}_{t+1,n}(\boldsymbol{\theta})=\sum_{j=1}^{m}\lambda^{(j)}_{t+1,n}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{)}q_{t+1}^{(j)}(\boldsymbol{\theta})G_{t+1}^{(k,j)}(\boldsymbol{\theta})+\kappa^{(j)}_{t+1,\infty}(\boldsymbol{\theta})=0\quad a.s.,

and the inductive proof is complete.

∎

Proof of Proposition
3
.

Fix any
\boldsymbol{\theta}\in\Theta
. We proceed by induction to show that for all
t\geq 1
,

n^{-1}\bar{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}_{t,n}(\boldsymbol{\theta})\underset{a.s.}{\overset{\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}}{\longrightarrow}}\bar{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}_{t,\infty}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*},\boldsymbol{\theta}),

with the other claims of the proposition proved along the way.

Using assumption
4
we have:

{n^{-1}\bar{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}_{0,n}(\boldsymbol{\theta})=n^{-1}{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}_{0,n}(\boldsymbol{\theta})\underset{a.s.}{\overset{\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}}{\longrightarrow}}{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}_{0,\infty}(\boldsymbol{\theta})=\boldsymbol{\lambda}_{0,\infty}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*},\boldsymbol{\theta})}.

Now, for
t\geq 1
assume that
{n^{-1}\bar{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}_{t-1,n}(\boldsymbol{\theta})\underset{a.s.}{\overset{\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}}{\longrightarrow}}\bar{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}_{t-1,\infty}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*},\boldsymbol{\theta})}
. We have:

\displaystyle n^{-1}\boldsymbol{\lambda}_{t,n}(\boldsymbol{\theta})

\displaystyle=\left[(n^{-1}\bar{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}_{t-1,n}(\boldsymbol{\theta})\odot\boldsymbol{\delta}_{t}(\boldsymbol{\theta}))^{\top}\mathbf{K}_{t,\boldsymbol{\eta}(\bar{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}_{t-1,n}(\boldsymbol{\theta})\odot\boldsymbol{\delta}_{t}(\boldsymbol{\theta}))}\right]^{\top}+n^{-1}\boldsymbol{\alpha}_{t,n}(\boldsymbol{\theta})

\displaystyle\underset{a.s.}{\overset{\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}}{\longrightarrow}}\left[(\bar{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}_{t-1,\infty}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*},\boldsymbol{\theta})\odot\boldsymbol{\delta}_{t}(\boldsymbol{\theta}))^{\top}\mathbf{K}_{t,\boldsymbol{\eta}(\bar{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}_{t-1,\infty}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*},\boldsymbol{\theta})\odot\boldsymbol{\delta}_{t}(\boldsymbol{\theta}))}\right]^{\top}+\boldsymbol{\alpha}_{t,\infty}(\boldsymbol{\theta})

\displaystyle=\boldsymbol{\lambda}_{t,\infty}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*},\boldsymbol{\theta}),

by the continuous mapping theorem (CMT) and assumptions
2
and
3
. A further application of the CMT and assumption
2
yields:

\displaystyle n^{-1}\boldsymbol{\mu}_{t,n}\left(\boldsymbol{\theta}\right)

\displaystyle=\left[\left(n^{-1}\boldsymbol{\lambda}_{t,n}(\boldsymbol{\theta})\odot\mathbf{q}_{t}(\boldsymbol{\theta})\right)^{\top}\mathbf{G}_{t}(\boldsymbol{\theta})\right]^{\top}+n^{-1}\boldsymbol{\kappa}_{t,n}(\boldsymbol{\theta})

\displaystyle\underset{a.s.}{\overset{\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}}{\longrightarrow}}\left[\left(\boldsymbol{\lambda}_{t,\infty}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*},\boldsymbol{\theta})\odot\mathbf{q}_{t}(\boldsymbol{\theta})\right)^{\top}\mathbf{G}_{t}(\boldsymbol{\theta})\right]^{\top}+\boldsymbol{\kappa}_{t,\infty}(\boldsymbol{\theta})

\displaystyle=\boldsymbol{\mu}_{t,\infty}\left(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*},\boldsymbol{\theta}\right)

Recalling from remark
1
that
n^{-1}\mathbf{y}_{t}\underset{a.s.}{\overset{\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}}{\longrightarrow}}\boldsymbol{\mu}_{t,\infty}\left(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*},\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}\right)
and applying the CMT, we have:

\displaystyle n^{-1}\bar{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}_{t,n}(\boldsymbol{\theta})=

\displaystyle\bigg[\mathbf{1}_{m}-\mathbf{q}_{t}(\boldsymbol{\theta})

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\left(\left[n^{-1}\mathbf{y}_{t}\oslash n^{-1}\boldsymbol{\mu}_{t,n}(\boldsymbol{\theta})\right]^{\top}\left(\left[\mathbf{1}_{m}\otimes\mathbf{q}_{t}(\boldsymbol{\theta})\right)\odot\mathbf{G}_{t}(\boldsymbol{\theta})\right]^{\top}\right)^{\top}\bigg]\odot n^{-1}\boldsymbol{\lambda}_{t,n}(\boldsymbol{\theta})

\displaystyle\underset{a.s.}{\overset{\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}}{\longrightarrow}}

\displaystyle\bigg[\mathbf{1}_{m}-\mathbf{q}_{t}(\boldsymbol{\theta})

\displaystyle+\left(\left[\boldsymbol{\mu}_{t,\infty}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*},\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*})\oslash\boldsymbol{\mu}_{t,\infty}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*},\boldsymbol{\theta})\right]^{\top}\left(\left[\mathbf{1}_{m}\otimes\mathbf{q}_{t}(\boldsymbol{\theta})\right]\odot\mathbf{G}_{t}(\boldsymbol{\theta})^{\top}\right)\right)^{\top}\bigg]\odot\boldsymbol{\lambda}_{t,\infty}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*},\boldsymbol{\theta})

\displaystyle=\bar{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}_{t,\infty}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*},\boldsymbol{\theta})

We note this limit is almost surely well defined since by lemma
14
for any
i\in[m]
,

\mu^{(i)}_{t,\infty}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*},\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*})=0\iff\mu^{(i)}_{t,\infty}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*},\boldsymbol{\theta})=0

and by lemma
13
if
\mu^{(i)}_{t,n}(\boldsymbol{\theta})=0
with positive probability then
y_{t}=0
,
\mathbb{P}_{n}^{\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}}
-a.s. In both these cases we are working under the convention
\frac{0}{0}\coloneqq 0
.
∎

Lemma 16
.

Let assumptions
2
-
4
hold. For all
\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}\in\Theta
and
t\geq 1
the function
\boldsymbol{\theta}\mapsto\boldsymbol{\mu}_{t,\infty}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*},\boldsymbol{\theta})
is continuous on
\Theta
.

Proof.

Fix an arbitrary
\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}\in\Theta
. Note that
\boldsymbol{\bar{\lambda}}_{0,\infty}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*},\boldsymbol{\theta}):=\boldsymbol{\bar{\lambda}}_{0,\infty}(\boldsymbol{\theta})
is continuous by assumption
4
. We will now show that for any
t\geq 1
, continuity of
\boldsymbol{\bar{\lambda}}_{t-1,\infty}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*},\boldsymbol{\theta})
implies continuity of
\boldsymbol{\lambda}_{t,\infty}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*},\boldsymbol{\theta})
,
\boldsymbol{\mu}_{t,\infty}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*},\boldsymbol{\theta})
, and
\boldsymbol{\bar{\lambda}}_{t,\infty}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*},\boldsymbol{\theta})
, from which the claim of the lemma follows.

Henceforth assume that
\boldsymbol{\bar{\lambda}}_{t-1,\infty}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*},\boldsymbol{\theta})
is continuous and recall that by definition of
\boldsymbol{\lambda}_{t,\infty}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*},\boldsymbol{\theta})
,

\boldsymbol{\lambda}_{t,\infty}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*},\boldsymbol{\theta})\coloneqq\left[(\bar{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}_{t-1,\infty}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*},\boldsymbol{\theta})\odot\boldsymbol{\delta}_{t}(\boldsymbol{\theta}))^{\top}\mathbf{K}_{t,\boldsymbol{\eta}(\bar{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}_{t-1,\infty}\left(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*},\boldsymbol{\theta})\odot\boldsymbol{\delta}_{t}(\boldsymbol{\theta})\right)}(\boldsymbol{\theta})\right]^{\top}+\boldsymbol{\alpha}_{t,\infty}(\boldsymbol{\theta}).

(B.85)

Continuity of
\boldsymbol{\delta}_{t}(\boldsymbol{\theta})
and
\boldsymbol{\alpha}_{t,\infty}(\boldsymbol{\theta})
in
\boldsymbol{\theta}
holds directly by assumptions
2
and
4
. By assumption
3
we know that
\mathbf{K}_{t,\boldsymbol{\eta}}(\boldsymbol{\theta})
is continuous in
\boldsymbol{\theta}
and
\boldsymbol{\eta}
. Hence, to show continuity of
\boldsymbol{\lambda}_{t,\infty}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*},\boldsymbol{\theta})
we shall show that
\boldsymbol{\eta}(\bar{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}_{t-1,\infty}\left(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*},\boldsymbol{\theta})\odot\boldsymbol{\delta}_{t}(\boldsymbol{\theta})\right)
is continuous in
\boldsymbol{\theta}
. The function
\boldsymbol{\eta}:\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}^{m}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}^{m}
is continuous everywhere except at
\boldsymbol{0}_{m}
, we now show that, by virtue of our assumptions, this discontinuity is immaterial. Consider the two following cases:

•

There exists
\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\prime}\in\Theta
such that
\bar{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}_{t-1,\infty}\left(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*},\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\prime}\right)\odot\boldsymbol{\delta}_{t}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\prime})=\mathbf{0}_{m}
. In this case, by assumption
2
and lemma
14
we have that
\bar{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}_{t-1,\infty}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*},\boldsymbol{\theta})\odot\boldsymbol{\delta}_{t}(\boldsymbol{\theta})=\mathbf{0}_{m}
for all
\boldsymbol{\theta}\in\Theta
, from which it follows that
\boldsymbol{\eta}(\bar{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}_{t-1,\infty}\left(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*},\boldsymbol{\theta})\odot\boldsymbol{\delta}_{t}(\boldsymbol{\theta})\right)=\boldsymbol{0}_{m}
for all
\boldsymbol{\theta}\in\Theta
, so that the continuity of
\boldsymbol{\eta}(\bar{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}_{t-1,\infty}\left(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*},\boldsymbol{\theta})\odot\boldsymbol{\delta}_{t}(\boldsymbol{\theta})\right)
in
\boldsymbol{\theta}
on
\Theta
holds trivially;

•

For all
\boldsymbol{\theta}\in\Theta
,
\bar{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}_{t-1,\infty}\left(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*},\boldsymbol{\theta}\right)\odot\boldsymbol{\delta}_{t}(\boldsymbol{\theta})\neq\mathbf{0}_{m}
. In this case the continuity of
\boldsymbol{\eta}(\bar{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}_{t-1,\infty}\left(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*},\boldsymbol{\theta}\right)\odot\boldsymbol{\delta}_{t}(\boldsymbol{\theta}))
in
\boldsymbol{\theta}
on
\Theta
follows from the continuity of
\boldsymbol{\eta}
on
\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}^{m}\setminus\{\boldsymbol{0}_{m}\}
.

Hence,
\boldsymbol{\theta}\mapsto\boldsymbol{\lambda}_{t,\infty}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*},\boldsymbol{\theta})
is continuous. Recall that:

\boldsymbol{\mu}_{t,\infty}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*},\boldsymbol{\theta})\coloneqq\left[\left(\boldsymbol{\lambda}_{t,\infty}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*},\boldsymbol{\theta})\odot\mathbf{q}_{t}(\boldsymbol{\theta})\right)^{\top}\mathbf{G}_{t}(\boldsymbol{\theta})\right]^{\top}+\boldsymbol{\kappa}_{t,\infty}(\boldsymbol{\theta}).

Due to the continuity of
\lambda_{t,\infty}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*},\boldsymbol{\theta})
and assumption
2
, this is a composition of continuous functions and hence
\boldsymbol{\theta}\mapsto\boldsymbol{\mu}_{t,\infty}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*},\boldsymbol{\theta})
is itself continuous. Now consider

\displaystyle\bar{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}_{t,\infty}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*},\boldsymbol{\theta})

\displaystyle\coloneqq\bigg[\mathbf{1}_{m}-\mathbf{q}_{t}(\boldsymbol{\theta})

\displaystyle+\left(\left[\boldsymbol{\mu}_{t,\infty}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*},\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*})\oslash\boldsymbol{\mu}_{t,\infty}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*},\boldsymbol{\theta})\right]^{\top}\left(\left[\mathbf{1}_{m}\otimes\mathbf{q}_{t}(\boldsymbol{\theta})\right]\odot\mathbf{G}_{t}(\boldsymbol{\theta})^{\top}\right)\right)^{\top}\bigg]\odot\boldsymbol{\lambda}_{t,\infty}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*},\boldsymbol{\theta}).

Each component of this function is trivially continuous on
\Theta
except the
\boldsymbol{\mu}_{t,\infty}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*},\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*})\oslash\boldsymbol{\mu}_{t,\infty}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*},\boldsymbol{\theta})
term, we will now prove its continuity. By lemma
14
, for each
i\in[m]
we need only consider the two cases:

•

either
\mu^{(i)}_{t,\infty}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*},\boldsymbol{\theta})=0
for all
\boldsymbol{\theta}\in\Theta
, in which case we have by convention
\mu^{(i)}_{t,\infty}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*},\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*})/\mu^{(i)}_{t,\infty}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*},\boldsymbol{\theta})\coloneqq 0
, which is continuous; or

•

\mu^{(i)}_{t,\infty}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*},\boldsymbol{\theta})\neq 0
for all
\boldsymbol{\theta}\in\Theta
, in which case
\mu^{(i)}_{t,\infty}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*},\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*})/\mu^{(i)}_{t,\infty}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*},\boldsymbol{\theta})
is continuous.

Hence we have elementwise continuity of
\boldsymbol{\mu}_{t,\infty}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*},\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*})\oslash\boldsymbol{\mu}_{t,\infty}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*},\boldsymbol{\theta})
which gives us continuity of
\boldsymbol{\theta}\mapsto\bar{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}_{t,\infty}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*},\boldsymbol{\theta})
.

We have shown that continuity of
\boldsymbol{\theta}\mapsto\boldsymbol{\bar{\lambda}}_{t-1,\infty}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*},\boldsymbol{\theta})
on
\Theta
implies continuity of
\boldsymbol{\theta}\mapsto\boldsymbol{\lambda}_{t,\infty}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*},\boldsymbol{\theta})
,
\boldsymbol{\theta}\mapsto\boldsymbol{\mu}_{t,\infty}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*},\boldsymbol{\theta})
and
\boldsymbol{\theta}\mapsto\bar{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}_{t,\infty}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*},\boldsymbol{\theta})
on
\Theta
, which completes the proof.
∎

B.2.2
Case (II)

Define:

\boldsymbol{\bar{\lambda}}_{0,\infty}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*},\boldsymbol{\theta})\coloneqq\boldsymbol{\lambda}_{0,\infty}(\boldsymbol{\theta}),

(B.86)

and for
r=1,\dots,R
and
t=\tau_{r-1}+1,\dots,\tau_{r}-1
,

\displaystyle\boldsymbol{\Lambda}_{t,\infty}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*},\boldsymbol{\theta})

\displaystyle\coloneqq\left(\boldsymbol{\bar{\lambda}}_{\tau_{r}-1,\infty}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*},\boldsymbol{\theta})\otimes\mathbf{1}_{m}\right)\odot\mathbf{K}_{t,\boldsymbol{\eta}(\boldsymbol{\bar{\lambda}}_{t-1,\infty}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*},\boldsymbol{\theta}))}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*},\boldsymbol{\theta}),

\displaystyle\boldsymbol{\bar{\lambda}}_{t,\infty}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*},\boldsymbol{\theta})

\displaystyle\coloneqq(\mathbf{1}_{m}^{\top}\boldsymbol{\Lambda}_{t,\infty}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*},\boldsymbol{\theta}))^{\top},

and

\displaystyle\boldsymbol{\Lambda}_{\tau_{r},\infty}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*},\boldsymbol{\theta})

\displaystyle\coloneqq\left(\bar{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}_{\tau_{r}-1,\infty}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*},\boldsymbol{\theta})\otimes\mathbf{1}_{m}\right)\odot\mathbf{K}_{t,\boldsymbol{\eta}\left(\boldsymbol{\bar{\lambda}}{\tau_{r}-1,\infty}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*},\boldsymbol{\theta})\right)}(\boldsymbol{\theta}),

\displaystyle\mathbf{M}_{r,\infty}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*},\boldsymbol{\theta})

\displaystyle\coloneqq\sum_{s=\tau_{r-1}+1}^{\tau_{r}}\mathbf{\Lambda}_{s,\infty}(\boldsymbol{\theta})\odot\mathbf{Q}_{s}(\boldsymbol{\theta}),

\displaystyle\bar{\boldsymbol{\Lambda}}_{\tau_{r},\infty}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*},\boldsymbol{\theta})

\displaystyle\coloneqq\left[\mathbf{1}_{m}\otimes\mathbf{1}_{m}-\mathbf{Q}_{\tau_{r}}(\boldsymbol{\theta})\right]\odot\boldsymbol{\Lambda}_{\tau_{r}}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*},\boldsymbol{\theta})

\displaystyle+\left[\mathbf{M}_{r,\infty}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*},\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*})\oslash\mathbf{M}_{r,\infty}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*},\boldsymbol{\theta})\right]\odot\left[\boldsymbol{\Lambda}_{\tau_{r},\infty}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*},\boldsymbol{\theta})\odot\mathbf{Q}_{\tau_{r}}(\boldsymbol{\theta})\right],

\displaystyle\bar{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}_{\tau_{r},\infty}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*},\boldsymbol{\theta})

\displaystyle\coloneqq(\mathbf{1}_{m}^{\top}\bar{\boldsymbol{\Lambda}}_{\tau_{r},\infty}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*},\boldsymbol{\theta}))^{\top}.

(B.87)

where if we encounter
0/0
in the element-wise division operation we set the entry to
0
by convention. The main result of section
B.2.2
is proposition
4
concerning the convergence to the above of the associated finite-
n
quantities computed using algorithm
3
.

Proposition 4
.

Let assumptions
2
-
4
hold. For any
\boldsymbol{\theta}\in\Theta
and
r\geq 1
and
t\geq 1
:

\displaystyle n^{-1}\mathbf{M}_{r,n}(\boldsymbol{\theta})

\displaystyle\underset{a.s.}{\overset{\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}}{\longrightarrow}}\mathbf{M}_{r,\infty}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*},\boldsymbol{\theta}),

\displaystyle n^{-1}\boldsymbol{\Lambda}_{t,n}(\boldsymbol{\theta})

\displaystyle\underset{a.s.}{\overset{\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}}{\longrightarrow}}\mathbf{\Lambda}_{t,\infty}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*},\boldsymbol{\theta}),

The proof is postponed until later in section
B.2.2
.

Remark 2
.

Similarly to properties of case (I) pointed out in remark
1
, by writing out the above definitions it can be checked that
\mathbf{N}_{t}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*})=\boldsymbol{\Lambda}_{t,\infty}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*},\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*})
, thus
n^{-1}\mathbf{Z}_{t}\underset{a.s.}{\overset{\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}}{\longrightarrow}}\boldsymbol{\Lambda}_{t,\infty}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*},\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*})
; and that
\mathbf{M}_{r,\infty}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*},\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*})
is equal to the right hand side of (
B.72
), thus
n^{-1}\bar{\mathbf{Y}}_{r}\underset{a.s.}{\overset{\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}}{\longrightarrow}}\mathbf{M}_{r,\infty}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*},\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*})
.

Similarly to as in section
B.2.1
, in order to prove proposition
4
we need to check that certain quantities are almost surely well defined. For the update step of algorithm
3
to be
\mathbb{P}^{\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}}
-a.s. well defined for all
\boldsymbol{\theta}\in\Theta
we need that if
M_{r,n}^{(i,j)}(\boldsymbol{\theta})=0
occurs with positive probability then
\bar{Y}_{r}^{(i,j)}=0

\mathbb{P}^{\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}}
- a.s. This is established in the following lemma.

Lemma 17
.

Let assumptions
2
-
4
hold. For any
\boldsymbol{\theta}\in\Theta
,
n\in\mathbb{N}
,
(i,j)\in[m]^{2}
and
r=1,\dots,R
:

\mathbb{P}_{n}^{\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}}\left(M^{(i,j)}_{r,n}(\boldsymbol{\theta})=0\right)>0\implies\bar{Y}_{r}^{(i,j)}=0,\quad\mathbb{P}_{n}^{\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}}a.s.

Proof.

Fix arbitrary
\boldsymbol{\theta}\in\Theta
and
n\in\mathbb{N}
. All almost sure statements made throughout the proof are with respect to
\mathbb{P}_{n}^{\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}}
. We will prove by induction that for all
r=1,\dots,R
we have that for all
s\in\{\tau_{r-1}+1,\dots,\tau_{r}\}
and
(i,j)\in[m]^{2}
, the following two implications hold.

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{n}^{\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}}\left(\Lambda_{s,n}^{(i,j)}(\boldsymbol{\theta})=0\right)>0\implies Z_{s}^{(i,j)}=0,\quad a.s.,

(B.88)

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{n}^{\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}}\left(M^{(i,j)}_{r,n}(\boldsymbol{\theta})=0\right)>0\implies\bar{Y}_{r}^{(i,j)}=0\quad a.s.

(B.89)

Consider the case
r=1
. We will first show that for all
s\in\{\tau_{0}+1,\dots,\tau_{1}\}
if, for some
(i,j)\in[m]^{2}
,
\mathbb{P}_{n}^{\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}}\left(\Lambda_{s,n}^{(i,j)}(\boldsymbol{\theta})=0\right)>0
then
Z_{s}^{(i,j)}=0
a.s. by induction on
s
. Suppose that for some
(i,j)\in[m]^{2}
,
\mathbb{P}_{n}^{\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}}\left(\Lambda_{1,\infty}^{(i,j)}(\boldsymbol{\theta})=0\right)>0
, i.e,

\mathbb{P}_{n}^{\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}}\left(\lambda_{0,n}^{(i)}(\boldsymbol{\theta})K^{(i,j)}_{1,\boldsymbol{\eta}(\boldsymbol{\lambda}_{0,n}(\boldsymbol{\theta}))}(\boldsymbol{\theta})=0\right)>0.

This implies that either:

•

\mathbb{P}_{n}^{\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}}\left(\lambda^{(i)}_{0,n}(\boldsymbol{\theta})=0\right)>0
, which since
\lambda^{(i)}_{0,n}(\boldsymbol{\theta})
is deterministic implies that

\lambda^{(i)}_{0,n}(\boldsymbol{\theta})=0
which by assumption
4
implies that
\lambda^{(i)}_{0,n}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*})=0\implies x^{(i)}_{0}=0
a.s.; or

•

K^{(i,j)}_{1,\boldsymbol{\eta}(\boldsymbol{\lambda}_{0,n}^{(i)})}(\boldsymbol{\theta})=0
, which implies
K^{(i,j)}_{1,\boldsymbol{\eta}(\mathbf{x}_{0})}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*})=0
by assumptions
2
,
3
, and
4
.

Together this implies imply
Z^{(i,j)}_{1}=0
a.s.. Now let
s\in\{\tau_{0}+1,\dots,\tau_{1}\}
and assume that if, for some
(i,j)\in[m]^{2}
,
\mathbb{P}_{n}^{\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}}\left(\Lambda_{s-1,n}^{(i,j)}(\boldsymbol{\theta})=0\right)>0
then
Z_{s-1}^{(i,j)}=0
a.s.. Now suppose for some
(i,j)\in[m]^{2}
,
\mathbb{P}_{n}^{\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}}\left(\Lambda_{s,n}^{(i,j)}(\boldsymbol{\theta})=0\right)>0
, i.e.,

\mathbb{P}_{n}^{\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}}\left(\left(\mathbf{1}_{m}^{\top}\boldsymbol{\Lambda}_{s-1,n}^{(\cdot,i)}(\boldsymbol{\theta})\right)K^{(i,j)}_{1,\boldsymbol{\eta}\left(\mathbf{1}_{m}^{\top}\boldsymbol{\Lambda}_{s-1,n}^{(\cdot,i)}(\boldsymbol{\theta})\right)}(\boldsymbol{\theta})=0\right)>0,

This implies that either:

•

\mathbb{P}_{n}^{\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}}\left(\mathbf{1}_{m}^{\top}\boldsymbol{\Lambda}_{0,n}^{(\cdot,i)}(\boldsymbol{\theta})=0\right)>0
, which by the induction hypothesis implies
\mathbf{1}_{m}^{\top}\mathbf{Z}_{s-1}^{(\cdot,i)}=0

a.s., which in turn implies
x^{(i)}_{s-1}=0
a.s.; or

•

\mathbb{P}_{n}^{\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}}\left(K^{(i,j)}_{1,\boldsymbol{\eta}\left(\mathbf{1}_{m}^{\top}\boldsymbol{\Lambda}_{s-1,n}^{(\cdot,i)}(\boldsymbol{\theta})\right)}(\boldsymbol{\theta})=0\right)>0
which by assumptions
2
and
3
and the induction hypothesis implies
K^{(i,j)}_{1,\boldsymbol{\eta}(\mathbf{x}_{s-1})}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*})=0
a.s.,

which together imply
Z^{(i,j)}_{s}=0
a.s.. Now suppose for some
(i,j)\in[m]^{2}
,
\mathbb{P}_{n}^{\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}}\left(M^{(i,j)}_{1,n}(\boldsymbol{\theta})=0\right)>0
, i.e.,

\mathbb{P}_{n}^{\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}}\left(\sum_{s=\tau_{0}+1}^{\tau_{1}}\Lambda^{(i,j)}_{s,n}(\boldsymbol{\theta})\odot Q^{(i,j)}_{s}(\boldsymbol{\theta})=0\right)>0,

then for all
s=\tau_{0}+1,\dots,\tau_{1}
either:

•

\mathbb{P}_{n}^{\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}}\left(\Lambda^{(i,j)}_{s,n}(\boldsymbol{\theta})=0\right)>0
, which implies
Z^{(i,j)}_{s}=0
hence
Y^{(i,j)}_{s}=0
a.s.; or

•

Q^{(i,j)}_{s}(\boldsymbol{\theta})=0
which by assumption
2
implies
Q^{(i,j)}_{s}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*})=0
hence
Y^{(i,j)}_{s}=0
a.s.,

and hence
\bar{Y}_{1}^{(i,j)}=\sum_{s=\tau_{0}+1}^{\tau_{1}}Y^{(i,j)}_{s}=0
a.s., this completes the proof of (
B.88
) and (
B.89
) for
r=1
.

For the induction hypothesis, suppose that (
B.88
) and (
B.89
) hold for some
r\geq 1
. Notice that:

\displaystyle\bar{\Lambda}^{(i,j)}_{\tau_{r},n}(\boldsymbol{\theta})=\left[1-Q^{(i,j)}_{\tau_{r}}(\boldsymbol{\theta})\right]\Lambda^{(i,j)}_{\tau_{r},n}(\boldsymbol{\theta})+\frac{\bar{Y}^{(i,j)}_{r}}{M^{(i,j)}_{r,n}(\boldsymbol{\theta})}\left[\Lambda^{(i,j)}_{\tau_{r},n}(\boldsymbol{\theta})\odot Q^{(i,j)}_{\tau_{r}}(\boldsymbol{\theta})\right]=0

is almost surely well defined by the induction hypothesis since we divide positive
\bar{Y}^{(i,j)}_{r}
by
0
with probability 0. Now suppose, for some
(i,j)\in[m]
, that
{\mathbb{P}_{n}^{\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}}\left(\bar{\Lambda}^{(i,j)}_{\tau_{r},n}(\boldsymbol{\theta})=0\right)>0}
, then either:

•

Q^{(i,j)}_{\tau_{r}}(\boldsymbol{\theta})<1
, which implies
\mathbb{P}_{n}^{\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}}\left(\Lambda^{(i,j)}_{\tau_{r},n}(\boldsymbol{\theta})=0\right)>0
, so that the first term of the sum is
0
with positive probability, which then implies
Z^{(i,j)}_{\tau_{r}}=0\quad a.s.
by the induction hypothesis; or

•

Q^{(i,j)}_{\tau_{r}}(\boldsymbol{\theta})=1
, which implies
\mathbb{P}_{n}^{\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}}\left(\Lambda^{(i,j)}_{\tau_{r},n}(\boldsymbol{\theta})=0\right)>0
, so that the second term in the sum is
0
with positive probability, which then implies
Z^{(i,j)}_{\tau_{r}}=0
a.s. by the induction hypothesis.

Using this and identical reasoning to that in the
r=1
case completes the induction.
∎

If, for some
i,j\in[m]
,
M^{(i,j)}_{r,\infty}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*},\boldsymbol{\theta})=0
and
M^{(i,j)}_{r,\infty}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*},\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*})>0
, then
\bar{\boldsymbol{\Lambda}}_{\tau_{r},\infty}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*},\boldsymbol{\theta})
would involve division of a finite number by zero. The following lemma implies this situation does not arise.

Lemma 18
.

Let assumptions
2
-
4
hold. Then for all
\boldsymbol{\theta},\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\prime}\in\Theta
,
(i,j)\in[m]^{2}
and
r=1,\dots,R
:

M^{(i,j)}_{r,\infty}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*},\boldsymbol{\theta})=0\iff M^{(i,j)}_{r,\infty}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*},\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\prime})=0.

Proof.

It is enough to establish the implication in one direction for arbitrary
\boldsymbol{\theta},\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\prime}\in\Theta
. We will prove by induction that for all
r=1,\dots,R
,
s=\tau_{r-1}+1,\dots,\tau_{r}
and
i,j\in[m]
,

\displaystyle\Lambda_{s,\infty}^{(i,j)}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*},\boldsymbol{\theta})=0

\displaystyle\implies\Lambda_{s,\infty}^{(i,j)}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*},\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\prime})=0,

\displaystyle M^{(i,j)}_{r,\infty}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*},\boldsymbol{\theta})=0

\displaystyle\implies M^{(i,j)}_{r,\infty}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*},\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\prime})=0.

Consider the case
r=1
. We will first show that for all
s\in\{\tau_{0}+1,\dots,\tau_{1}\}
,
\Lambda_{s,\infty}^{(i,j)}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*},\boldsymbol{\theta})=0\implies\Lambda_{s,\infty}^{(i,j)}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*},\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\prime})=0
by induction on
s
. To this end suppose that for some
(i,j)\in[m]^{2}
:

\Lambda_{1,\infty}^{(i,j)}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*},\boldsymbol{\theta})=\lambda_{0,\infty}^{(i)}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*},\boldsymbol{\theta})K^{(i,j)}_{1,\boldsymbol{\eta}(\boldsymbol{\lambda}_{0,\infty}^{(i)}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*},\boldsymbol{\theta}))}(\boldsymbol{\theta})=0.

Then either:

•

\lambda^{(i)}_{0,\infty}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*},\boldsymbol{\theta})=\lambda^{(i)}_{0,n}(\boldsymbol{\theta})=0
, which by assumption
4
implies
\lambda_{0,n}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\prime})=\lambda^{(i)}_{0,\infty}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*},\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\prime})=0
; or

•

K^{(i,j)}_{1,\boldsymbol{\eta}(\lambda_{0,\infty}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*},\boldsymbol{\theta})}(\boldsymbol{\theta})=0
, which by assumptions
2
,
3
, and
4
implies
K^{(i,j)}_{1,\boldsymbol{\eta}(\lambda_{0,\infty}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*},\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\prime})}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\prime})=0
.

Hence:

\Lambda_{1,\infty}^{(i,j)}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*},\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\prime})=\lambda_{0,\infty}^{(i)}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*},\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\prime})K^{(i,j)}_{1,\boldsymbol{\eta}(\boldsymbol{\lambda}_{0,\infty}^{(i)}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*},\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\prime}))}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\prime})=0.

Now assume that for
s=tau_{0}+1,\dots,\tau_{1}
that
\Lambda_{s-1,\infty}^{(i,j)}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*},\boldsymbol{\theta})=0\implies\Lambda_{s-1,\infty}^{(i,j)}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*},\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\prime})=0
, then if:

\Lambda_{s,\infty}^{(i,j)}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*},\boldsymbol{\theta})=\left(\mathbf{1}_{m}^{\top}\boldsymbol{\Lambda}_{s-1,\infty}^{(\cdot,i)}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*},\boldsymbol{\theta})\right)K^{(i,j)}_{1,\boldsymbol{\eta}\left(\mathbf{1}_{m}^{\top}\boldsymbol{\Lambda}_{s-1,\infty}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*},\boldsymbol{\theta})\right)}(\boldsymbol{\theta})=0,

we must have either:

•

\left(\mathbf{1}_{m}^{\top}\boldsymbol{\Lambda}_{s-1,\infty}^{(\cdot,i)}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*},\boldsymbol{\theta})\right)=0
, which by the induction hypothesis implies
\left(\mathbf{1}_{m}^{\top}\boldsymbol{\Lambda}_{s-1,\infty}^{(\cdot,i)}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*},\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\prime})\right)=0
; or

•

K^{(i,j)}_{1,\boldsymbol{\eta}\left(\mathbf{1}_{m}^{\top}\boldsymbol{\Lambda}_{s-1,\infty}^{(\cdot,i)}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*},\boldsymbol{\theta})\right)}(\boldsymbol{\theta})=0
, which by the above and assumptions
2
and
3
implies

K^{(i,j)}_{1,\boldsymbol{\eta}\left(\mathbf{1}_{m}^{\top}\boldsymbol{\Lambda}_{s-1,\infty}^{(\cdot,i)}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*},\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\prime})\right)}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\prime})=0
.

We therefore find:

\Lambda_{s,\infty}^{(i,j)}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*},\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\prime})=\left(\mathbf{1}_{m}^{\top}\boldsymbol{\Lambda}_{s-1,\infty}^{(\cdot,i)}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*},\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\prime})\right)K^{(i,j)}_{1,\boldsymbol{\eta}\left(\mathbf{1}_{m}^{\top}\boldsymbol{\Lambda}_{s-1,\infty}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*},\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\prime})\right)}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\prime})=0.

completing the intermediary induction on
s
.
Now consider:

\displaystyle M^{(i,j)}_{1,\infty}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*},\boldsymbol{\theta})

\displaystyle=\sum_{s=1}^{\tau_{1}}\Lambda^{(i,j)}_{s,\infty}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*},\boldsymbol{\theta})\odot Q^{(i,j)}_{s}(\boldsymbol{\theta})=0,

then for all
s=\tau_{0}+1,\dots,\tau_{1}
either:

•

\Lambda^{(i,j)}_{s,\infty}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*},\boldsymbol{\theta})=0\implies\Lambda^{(i,j)}_{s,\infty}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*},\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\prime})=0
; or

•

Q^{(i,j)}_{s}(\boldsymbol{\theta})=0\implies Q^{(i,j)}_{s}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\prime})=0
,

and hence:

\displaystyle M^{(i,j)}_{1,\infty}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*},\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\prime})

\displaystyle=\sum_{s=1}^{\tau_{1}}\Lambda^{(i,j)}_{s,\infty}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*},\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\prime})\odot Q^{(i,j)}_{s}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\prime})=0,

completing the
r=1
case.

Now assume that for all
s\in\{\tau_{r-1}+1,\dots,\tau_{r}\}
that
\Lambda_{s,\infty}^{(i,j)}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*},\boldsymbol{\theta})=0\implies\Lambda_{s,\infty}^{(i,j)}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*},\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\prime})=0
and that
M^{(i,j)}_{r,\infty}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*},\boldsymbol{\theta})=0\implies M^{(i,j)}_{r,\infty}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*},\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\prime})=0
. Then we have that if:

\displaystyle\bar{\Lambda}^{(i,j)}_{\tau_{r},\infty}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*},\boldsymbol{\theta})

\displaystyle=\left[1-Q^{(i,j)}_{\tau_{r}}(\boldsymbol{\theta})\right]\odot\Lambda^{(i,j)}_{\tau_{r},\infty}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*},\boldsymbol{\theta})

\displaystyle+\frac{M^{(i,j)}_{r,\infty}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*},\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*})}{M^{(i,j)}_{r,\infty}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*},\boldsymbol{\theta})}\odot\left[\Lambda^{(i,j)}_{\tau_{r},\infty}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*},\boldsymbol{\theta})\odot Q^{(i,j)}_{\tau_{r}}(\boldsymbol{\theta})\right]=0,

which is well defined by the inductive hypothesis, then either:

•

Q^{(i,j)}_{\tau_{r}}(\boldsymbol{\theta})<1
, and then
\Lambda^{(i,j)}_{\tau_{r},\infty}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*},\boldsymbol{\theta})=0\implies\Lambda^{(i,j)}_{\tau_{r},\infty}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*},\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\prime})\implies\bar{\Lambda}^{(i,j)}_{\tau_{r},\infty}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*},\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\prime})=0
, hence the first term is
0
, or

•

Q^{(i,j)}_{\tau_{r}}(\boldsymbol{\theta})=1
, and then
\Lambda^{(i,j)}_{\tau_{r}}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*},\boldsymbol{\theta})=0\implies\Lambda^{(i,j)}_{\tau_{r},\infty}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*},\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\prime})\implies\bar{\Lambda}^{(i,j)}_{\tau_{r},\infty}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*},\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\prime})=0
, so that the right hand term is
0
.

This along with using the same reasoning used in the
r=1
case gives:

\Lambda_{\tau_{r},\infty}^{(i,j)}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*},\boldsymbol{\theta})=\left(\mathbf{1}_{m}^{\top}\boldsymbol{\bar{\Lambda}}_{\tau_{r}-1,\infty}^{(\cdot,i)}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*},\boldsymbol{\theta})\right)K^{(i,j)}_{1,\boldsymbol{\eta}\left(\mathbf{1}_{m}^{\top}\boldsymbol{\bar{\Lambda}}_{\tau_{r}-1,\infty}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*},\boldsymbol{\theta})\right)}(\boldsymbol{\theta})=0,

implies

\Lambda_{\tau_{r},\infty}^{(i,j)}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*},\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\prime})=\left(\mathbf{1}_{m}^{\top}\boldsymbol{\bar{\Lambda}}_{\tau_{r}-1,\infty}^{(\cdot,i)}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*},\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\prime})\right)K^{(i,j)}_{1,\boldsymbol{\eta}\left(\mathbf{1}_{m}^{\top}\boldsymbol{\bar{\Lambda}}_{\tau_{r}-1,\infty}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*},\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\prime})\right)}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\prime})=0.

Using this and further using identical inductive reasoning to the
r=1
case we see that for all
s=\tau_{r}+1,\dots,\tau_{r+1}
,
\Lambda_{s,\infty}^{(i,j)}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*},\boldsymbol{\theta})=0\implies\Lambda_{s,\infty}^{(i,j)}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*},\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\prime})=0
and further that
M^{(i,j)}_{r+1,\infty}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*},\boldsymbol{\theta})=0\implies M^{(i,j)}_{r+1,\infty}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*},\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\prime})=0
. This completes the inductive proof.
∎

The following lemma is used in the proof of lemma
6
.

Lemma 19
.

Let assumptions
2
-
4
hold. For all
\boldsymbol{\theta}\in\Theta
,
n\in\mathbb{N}
,
(i,j)\in[m]^{2}
, and
r\in\{1,\dots,R\}
:

M^{(i,j)}_{r,\infty}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*},\boldsymbol{\theta})=0\implies M^{(i,j)}_{r,n}(\boldsymbol{\theta})=0,\quad\mathbb{P}_{n}^{\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}}\text{-}a.s.

Proof.

Fix arbitrary
\boldsymbol{\theta}\in\Theta
and
n\in\mathbb{N}
. We will prove that for all
r=1,\dots,R
,
s=\tau_{r-1}+1,\dots,\tau_{r}
and
i,j\in[m]
the following two implications hold:

\displaystyle\Lambda_{s,\infty}^{(i,j)}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*},\boldsymbol{\theta})=0

\displaystyle\implies\Lambda_{s,n}^{(i,j)}(\boldsymbol{\theta})=0,\quad a.s.

\displaystyle M^{(i,j)}_{r,\infty}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*},\boldsymbol{\theta})=0

\displaystyle\implies M^{(i,j)}_{r,n}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*},\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\prime})=0,\quad a.s.

The induction is on
r
and
s
. Consider
r=1
. We will first show that for all
s\in\tau_{0}+1,\dots,\tau_{1}
that
\Lambda_{s,\infty}^{(i,j)}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*},\boldsymbol{\theta})=0\implies\Lambda_{s,\infty}^{(i,j)}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*},\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\prime})=0
by induction on
s
. We have for
t-1
case:

\Lambda_{1,\infty}^{(i,j)}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*},\boldsymbol{\theta})=\lambda_{0,\infty}^{(i)}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*},\boldsymbol{\theta})K^{(i,j)}_{1,\boldsymbol{\eta}(\boldsymbol{\lambda}_{0,\infty}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*},\boldsymbol{\theta}))}(\boldsymbol{\theta})=0,

which implies that either:

•

\lambda^{(i)}_{0,\infty}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*},\boldsymbol{\theta})=\lambda^{(i)}_{0,\infty}(\boldsymbol{\theta})=0
, in which case
\lambda^{(i)}_{0,n}(\boldsymbol{\theta})=0
or

•

K^{(i,j)}_{1,\boldsymbol{\eta}(\lambda_{0,\infty}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*},\boldsymbol{\theta})}(\boldsymbol{\theta})=0
, in which case
K^{(i,j)}_{1,\boldsymbol{\eta}(\boldsymbol{\lambda}_{0,n}(\boldsymbol{\theta}))}(\boldsymbol{\theta})=0
,

so that:

\Lambda_{1,n}^{(i,j)}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*},\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\prime})=\lambda^{(i)}_{0,n}(\boldsymbol{\theta}))K^{(i,j)}_{1,\boldsymbol{\eta}(\boldsymbol{\lambda}_{0,n}(\boldsymbol{\theta}))}(\boldsymbol{\theta})=0.

Now assume that given
\Lambda_{s-1,\infty}^{(i,j)}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*},\boldsymbol{\theta})=0\implies\Lambda_{s-1,n}^{(i,j)}(\boldsymbol{\theta})=0\quad a.s.
, then:

\Lambda_{s,\infty}^{(i,j)}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*},\boldsymbol{\theta})=\left(\mathbf{1}_{m}^{\top}\boldsymbol{\Lambda}_{s-1,\infty}^{(\cdot,i)}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*},\boldsymbol{\theta})\right)K^{(i,j)}_{1,\boldsymbol{\eta}\left(\mathbf{1}_{m}^{\top}\boldsymbol{\Lambda}_{s-1,\infty}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*},\boldsymbol{\theta})\right)}(\boldsymbol{\theta})=0,

which in turn implies either:

•

\left(\mathbf{1}_{m}^{\top}\boldsymbol{\Lambda}_{s-1,\infty}^{(\cdot,i)}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*},\boldsymbol{\theta})\right)=0_{m}
, which implies
\left(\mathbf{1}_{m}^{\top}\boldsymbol{\Lambda}_{s-1,n}^{(\cdot,i)}(\boldsymbol{\theta})\right)=0
a.s.; or

•

K^{(i,j)}_{1,\boldsymbol{\eta}\left(\mathbf{1}_{m}^{\top}\boldsymbol{\Lambda}_{s-1,\infty}^{(\cdot,i)}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*},\boldsymbol{\theta})\right)}(\boldsymbol{\theta})=0
, which implies
K^{(i,j)}_{1,\boldsymbol{\eta}\left(\mathbf{1}_{m}^{\top}\boldsymbol{\Lambda}_{s-1,n}(\boldsymbol{\theta})\right)}(\boldsymbol{\theta})=0
a.s..

Together we find:

\Lambda_{s,n}^{(i,j)}(\boldsymbol{\theta})=\left(\mathbf{1}_{m}^{\top}\boldsymbol{\Lambda}_{s-1,n}^{(\cdot,i)}(\boldsymbol{\theta})\right)K^{(i,j)}_{1,\boldsymbol{\eta}\left(\mathbf{1}_{m}^{\top}\boldsymbol{\Lambda}_{s-1,n}(\boldsymbol{\theta})\right)}(\boldsymbol{\theta})=0,

completing the intermediary induction on
s
. Now consider:

\displaystyle M^{(i,j)}_{1,\infty}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*},\boldsymbol{\theta})

\displaystyle=\sum_{s=1}^{\tau_{1}}\Lambda^{(i,j)}_{s,\infty}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*},\boldsymbol{\theta})\odot Q^{(i,j)}_{s}(\boldsymbol{\theta})=0,

then for all
s=\tau_{0}+1,\dots,\tau_{1}
either

•

\Lambda^{(i,j)}_{s,\infty}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*},\boldsymbol{\theta})=0
, which implies
\Lambda^{(i,j)}_{s,n}(\boldsymbol{\theta})=0
; or

•

Q^{(i,j)}_{s}(\boldsymbol{\theta})=0
,

and hence:

\displaystyle M^{(i,j)}_{1,n}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*},\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\prime})
