Algorithm 8

Filtering for the age-structured model

1:

Initialize:

\boldsymbol{\bar{\lambda}}_{0}\leftarrow\boldsymbol{\lambda}_{0}
.

2:

\boldsymbol{\bar{\lambda}}_{0}\leftarrow\left[\boldsymbol{\bar{\lambda}}_{1,0}^{\top}\;\cdots\;\boldsymbol{\bar{\lambda}}_{4,0}^{\top}\right]^{\top}

3:

for

r\geq 1:

4:

for

t=\tau_{r-1}+1,\dots,\tau_{r}-1
:

5:

for

k=1,\dots,4
:

6:

\mathbf{\Lambda}_{k,t}\leftarrow(\boldsymbol{\bar{\lambda}}_{k,t-1}\otimes\mathbf{1}_{m})\odot\mathbf{K}_{k,t,{\boldsymbol{\eta}}\left(\boldsymbol{\bar{\lambda}}_{t-1}\right)}

7:

\bar{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}_{k,t}\leftarrow(\mathbf{1}_{m}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{\Lambda}}_{k,t})^{\top}

8:

end for

9:

\boldsymbol{\bar{\lambda}}_{t}\leftarrow\left[\boldsymbol{\bar{\lambda}}_{1,t}^{\top}\;\cdots\;\boldsymbol{\bar{\lambda}}_{4,t}^{\top}\right]^{\top}

10:

end for

11:

for

k=1,\dots,4
:

12:

\mathbf{\Lambda}_{k,\tau_{r}}\leftarrow(\boldsymbol{\bar{\lambda}}_{k,\tau_{r}-1}\otimes\mathbf{1}_{m})\odot\mathbf{K}_{k,\tau_{r},{\boldsymbol{\eta}}\left(\boldsymbol{\bar{\lambda}}_{t-1}\right)}

13:

\mathbf{M}_{k,r}\leftarrow\sum_{t=\tau_{r-1}}^{\tau_{r}}\boldsymbol{\Lambda}_{k,t}\odot\mathbf{Q}_{k,t}

14:

\mathbf{\bar{\Lambda}}_{k,\tau_{r}}\leftarrow\left(\mathbf{1}_{m}\otimes\mathbf{1}_{m}-\mathbf{Q}_{k,\tau_{r}}\right)\odot\bar{\boldsymbol{\Lambda}}_{k,\tau_{r}}+\bar{\mathbf{Y}}_{k,r}\odot\boldsymbol{\Lambda}_{k,\tau_{r}}\odot\mathbf{Q}_{k,\tau_{r}}\oslash\mathbf{M}_{k,r}

15:

\bar{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}_{k,\tau_{r}}\leftarrow(\mathbf{1}_{m}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{\Lambda}}_{k,\tau_{r}})^{\top}

16:

end for

17:

\boldsymbol{\bar{\lambda}}_{\tau_{r}}\leftarrow\left[\boldsymbol{\bar{\lambda}}_{1,\tau_{r}}^{\top}\;\cdots\;\boldsymbol{\bar{\lambda}}_{4,\tau_{r}}^{\top}\right]^{\top}

18:

{\mathcal{L}}(\bar{\mathbf{Y}}_{1:4,r}|\bar{\mathbf{Y}}_{1:4,1:r-1})\leftarrow\sum_{k=1}^{4}\mathbf{-}1_{m}^{\top}\mathbf{M}_{k,r}\mathbf{1}_{m}+\mathbf{1}_{m}^{\top}(\bar{\mathbf{Y}}_{k,r}\odot\mathbf{M}_{k,r})\mathbf{1}_{m}-\mathbf{1}_{m}^{\top}\log(\bar{\mathbf{Y}}_{k,r}!)\mathbf{1}_{m}

19:

end for

C.5
Supplementary material for section
7.3

C.5.1
Model

The evolution of the full age stratified rotavirus model at time
t
is given by:

\displaystyle S_{1,t+1}

\displaystyle=S_{1,t-1}+A_{1,t}+E_{1,t}-B_{1,t}-F_{1,t}^{(S)},

(C.5)

\displaystyle I_{1,t+1}

\displaystyle=I_{1,t}+B_{1,t}-C_{1,t}-F_{1,t}^{(I)},

(C.6)

\displaystyle R_{1,t+1}

\displaystyle=R_{1,t}+C_{1,t}-E_{1,t}-F_{1,t}^{(R)},

(C.7)

\displaystyle S_{2,t+1}

\displaystyle=S_{2,t}+F_{1,t}^{(S)}+E_{2,t}-B_{2,t}-F_{2,t}^{(S)},

(C.8)

\displaystyle I_{2,t+1}

\displaystyle=I_{2,t}+F_{1,t}^{(I)}+B_{2,t}-C_{2,t}-F_{2,t}^{(I)},

(C.9)

\displaystyle R_{2,t+1}

\displaystyle=R_{2,t}+F_{1,t}^{(R)}+C_{2,t}-E_{2,t}-F_{2,t}^{(R)},

(C.10)

\displaystyle S_{3,+1t}

\displaystyle=S_{3,t}+F_{2,t}^{(S)}+E_{3,t}-B_{3,t}-D_{t}^{(S)},

(C.11)

\displaystyle I_{3,t+1}

\displaystyle=I_{3,t}+F_{2,t}^{(I)}+B_{3,t}-C_{3,t}-D_{t}^{(I)},

(C.12)

\displaystyle R_{3,t+1}

\displaystyle=R_{3,t}+F_{2,t}^{(R)}+C_{3,t}-E_{3,t}-D_{t}^{(R)},

(C.13)

where at time
t
:
A_{1,t}\sim\text{Pois}(\alpha_{t})
, for some
\alpha_{t}\in\mathbb{R}
represents new births, which is chosen according to historical birth record data;
B_{\cdot,t}
represents new infectives;
C_{\cdot,t}
represents recovering individuals;
D_{t}^{\cdot}\sim\text{Binom}\left({\cdot_{t-1},1-\delta}\right)
represents emigrating (dying) individuals;
E_{\cdot,t}
represents individuals experiencing waning immunity; and
F_{\cdot,t}
represents ageing individuals.

\displaystyle\left[\begin{array}[]{c}B_{1,t}\\
F_{1,t}^{(S)}\\
S_{1,t}-B_{1,t}-F_{1,t}^{(S)}\end{array}\right]

\displaystyle\sim\text{Mult}\left(S_{1,t},\left[\begin{array}[]{c}p_{1,t}\\
1-e^{(-hd_{1})}\\
e^{(-hd_{1})}-p_{k,t}\end{array}\right]\right)

\displaystyle\left[\begin{array}[]{c}C_{1,t}\\
F_{1,t}^{(I)}\\
I_{1,t}-C_{1,t}-F_{1,t}^{(I)}\end{array}\right]

\displaystyle\sim\text{Mult}\left(I_{1,t},\left[\begin{array}[]{c}1-e^{-h\gamma}\\
1-e^{-hd_{1}}\\
e^{-h\gamma}+e^{-hd_{1}}-1\end{array}\right]\right)

\displaystyle\left[\begin{array}[]{c}E_{1,t}\\
F_{1,t}^{(R)}\\
R_{1,t}-E_{1,t}-F_{1,t}^{(R)}\end{array}\right]

\displaystyle\sim\text{Mult}\left(R_{1,t},\left[\begin{array}[]{c}1-e^{-h\omega}\\
1-e^{-hd_{1}}\\
e^{-h\omega}+e^{-hd_{1}}-1\end{array}\right]\right)

\displaystyle\left[\begin{array}[]{c}B_{2,t}\\
F_{2,t}^{(S)}\\
S_{2,t}-B_{2,t}-F_{2,t}^{(S)}\end{array}\right]

\displaystyle\sim\text{Mult}\left(S_{2,t},\left[\begin{array}[]{c}p_{2,t}\\
1-e^{(-hd_{2})}\\
e^{(-hd_{2})}-p_{2,t}\end{array}\right]\right)

\displaystyle\left[\begin{array}[]{c}C_{2,t}\\
F_{2,t}^{(I)}\\
I_{2,t}-C_{2,t}-F_{2,t}^{(I)}\end{array}\right]

\displaystyle\sim\text{Mult}\left(I_{2,t},\left[\begin{array}[]{c}1-e^{-h\gamma}\\
1-e^{-hd_{2}}\\
e^{-h\gamma}+e^{-hd_{2}}-1\end{array}\right]\right)

\displaystyle\left[\begin{array}[]{c}E_{2,t}\\
F_{2,t}^{(R)}\\
R_{2,t}-E_{2,t}-F_{2,t}^{(R)}\end{array}\right]

\displaystyle\sim\text{Mult}\left(R_{2,t},\left[\begin{array}[]{c}1-e^{-h\omega}\\
1-e^{-hd_{2}}\\
e^{-h\omega}+e^{-hd_{2}}-1\end{array}\right]\right)

\displaystyle\left[\begin{array}[]{c}B_{3,t}\\
S_{3,t}-D^{(S)}_{t}-B_{3,t}\end{array}\right]

\displaystyle\sim\text{Mult}\left(S_{3,t}-D^{(S)}_{t},\left[\begin{array}[]{c}p_{3,t}\\
1-p_{3,t}\end{array}\right]\right)

\displaystyle\left[\begin{array}[]{c}C_{3,t}\\
I_{3,t}-D_{t}^{(I)}-C_{2,t}\end{array}\right]

\displaystyle\sim\text{Mult}\left(I_{3,t}-D_{t}^{(I)},\left[\begin{array}[]{c}1-e^{-h\gamma}\\
e^{-h\gamma}\end{array}\right]\right)

\displaystyle\left[\begin{array}[]{c}E_{3,t}\\
R_{3,t}-D_{t}^{(R)}-E_{2,t}\end{array}\right]

\displaystyle\sim\text{Mult}\left(R_{3,t}-D_{t}^{(R)},\left[\begin{array}[]{c}1-e^{-h\omega}\\
e^{-h\omega}\end{array}\right]\right)

To align notation with the model descriptions in section
3
collect observations at time
r
in the matrix
\bar{\mathbf{Y}}_{r}\in\mathbb{N}^{9\times 9}
which has elements equal to zero except
\bar{Y}_{r}^{(3k-2,3k-1)}=Y_{r,k}
for age groups
k=1,2,3
, similarly collect reporting rates in
{\mathbf{Q}}_{r}\in\mathbb{N}^{9\times 9}
which has elements equal to zero except
Q_{r}^{(3k-2,3k-1)}=q_{r,k}
for
k=1,2,3
. Define
{\mathbf{x}}_{t}=[S_{1,t}\;I_{1,t}\;R_{1,t}\;S_{2,t}\;I_{2,t}\;R_{2,t}\;S_{3,t}\;I_{3,t}\;R_{3,t}]
. Identify the matrix:

\displaystyle\mathbf{K}_{t,\boldsymbol{\eta}}^{(1,\cdot)}

\displaystyle=\left[e^{-hd_{1}}-p_{1,t}\quad\quad p_{1,t}\quad\quad 0\quad\quad 1-e^{-hd_{1}}\quad\quad 0\quad\quad 0\quad\quad 0\quad\quad 0\quad\quad 0\right],

(C.63)

\displaystyle\mathbf{K}_{t,\boldsymbol{\eta}}^{(2,\cdot)}

\displaystyle=\left[0\quad\quad e^{-h\gamma}+e^{-hd_{1}}-1\quad\quad 1-e^{-h\gamma}\quad\quad 0\quad\quad 1-e^{-hd_{1}}\quad\quad 0\quad\quad 0\quad\quad 0\quad\quad 0\right],

(C.64)

\displaystyle\mathbf{K}_{t,\boldsymbol{\eta}}^{(3,\cdot)}

\displaystyle=\left[1-e^{-h\omega}\quad\quad 0\quad\quad 0\quad\quad e^{-h\omega}+e^{-hd_{1}}-1\quad\quad 0\quad\quad 1-e^{-hd_{1}}\quad\quad 0\quad\quad 0\quad\quad 0\right],

(C.65)

\displaystyle\mathbf{K}_{t,\boldsymbol{\eta}}^{(4,\cdot)}

\displaystyle=\left[0\quad\quad 0\quad\quad 0\quad\quad e^{-hd_{2}}-p_{2,t}\quad\quad p_{2,t}\quad\quad 0\quad\quad 1-e^{-hd_{2}}\quad\quad 0\quad\quad 0\right],

(C.66)

\displaystyle\mathbf{K}_{t,\boldsymbol{\eta}}^{(5,\cdot)}

\displaystyle=\left[0\quad\quad 0\quad\quad 0\quad\quad 0\quad\quad e^{-h\gamma}+e^{-hd_{2}}-1\quad\quad 1-e^{-h\gamma}\quad\quad 0\quad\quad 1-e^{-hd_{2}}\quad\quad 0\right],

(C.67)

\displaystyle\mathbf{K}_{t,\boldsymbol{\eta}}^{(6,\cdot)}

\displaystyle=\left[0\quad\quad 0\quad\quad 0\quad\quad 1-e^{-h\omega}\quad\quad 0\quad\quad e^{-h\omega}+e^{-hd_{1}}-1\quad\quad 0\quad\quad 0\quad\quad 1-e^{-hd_{1}}\right],

(C.68)

\displaystyle\mathbf{K}_{t,\boldsymbol{\eta}}^{(7,\cdot)}

\displaystyle=\left[0\quad\quad 0\quad\quad 0\quad\quad 0\quad\quad 0\quad\quad 0\quad\quad 1-p_{3,t}\quad\quad p_{3,t}\quad\quad 0\right],

(C.69)

\displaystyle\mathbf{K}_{t,\boldsymbol{\eta}}^{(8,\cdot)}

\displaystyle=\left[0\quad\quad 0\quad\quad 0\quad\quad 0\quad\quad 0\quad\quad 0\quad\quad 0\quad\quad e^{-h\gamma}\quad\quad 1-e^{-h\gamma}\right],

(C.70)

\displaystyle\mathbf{K}_{t,\boldsymbol{\eta}}^{(9,\cdot)}

\displaystyle=\left[0\quad\quad 0\quad\quad 0\quad\quad 0\quad\quad 0\quad\quad 0\quad\quad 1-e^{-h\omega}\quad\quad 0\quad\quad e^{-h\omega}\right].

(C.71)

Where for models EqEq and EqOv we have
p_{k,t}=1-\exp\left\{-\boldsymbol{\beta}_{k}^{\top}\frac{\boldsymbol{I}_{t}}{n}\chi_{t}\right\}
for
k=1,2,3
, and for model OvOv we have
p_{k,t}=1-\exp\left\{-\boldsymbol{\beta}_{k}^{\top}\frac{\boldsymbol{I}_{t}}{n}\chi_{t}\xi_{r}\right\}
for
k=1,2,3
, in which case we will write
\mathbf{K}_{t,\boldsymbol{\eta}}=\mathbf{K}_{t,\boldsymbol{\eta},\xi}
.

For models EqOv and OvOv we have for
k=1,2,3
:

Q_{r}^{(3k-2,3k-1)}\sim\mathcal{N}(\mu_{q},\sigma_{q}^{2})_{\geq 0,\leq 1}

corresponding to the reporting rate of new infectived individuals for each age group. Denote this prior density of
\mathbf{Q}_{r}
as
f(\cdot\mid\mu_{q},\sigma_{q}^{2})
.

C.5.2
Inference

We assume that the values of
\alpha,d_{1},d_{2},\delta,\gamma,\omega
and
\mu_{q}
are known, we set them to the same values as assumed in
[
63
]
, these are available on the GitHub page. All other parameters are to be estimated.

Laplace approximation proposals for the rotavirus example

Consider algorithm
9
. We factorise the proposal of particles at time
r
,
[\xi_{r}^{(i)},\mathbf{Q}_{r}^{(i)}]
, into sampling
\xi_{r}^{(i)}
from its prior, then given this we seek a Laplace/PAL approximation to the distribution:

\hat{p}(\mathbf{Q}_{r}\mid\bar{\mathbf{Y}}_{1:r},\mathbf{Q}_{1:r-1},\boldsymbol{\xi}_{1:r}):=\frac{\exp\mathcal{L}(\bar{\mathbf{Y}}_{r}\mid\bar{\mathbf{Y}}_{1:r-1},\mathbf{Q}_{1:r},\boldsymbol{\xi}_{1:r})f(\mathbf{Q}_{r}\mid\mu_{q},\sigma_{q}^{2})}{\int\exp\mathcal{L}(\bar{\mathbf{Y}}_{r}\mid\bar{\mathbf{Y}}_{1:r-1},\mathbf{Q}_{1:r},\boldsymbol{\xi}_{1:r})f(\mathbf{Q}_{r}\mid\mu_{q},\sigma_{q}^{2})d\mathbf{Q}_{r}}

Surpressing dependence on the particle, let
\mathbf{L}_{r}=\sum_{t=\tau_{r-1}+1}^{\tau_{r}}\boldsymbol{\Lambda}_{t}
with
\boldsymbol{\Lambda}_{t}
calculated as per line
6
of algorithm
9
, we have for some constants
C_{1},C_{2}
:

\displaystyle\log\hat{p}(\mathbf{Q}_{r}\mid\bar{\mathbf{Y}}_{1:r},\mathbf{Q}_{1:r-1},\boldsymbol{\xi}_{1:r})

\displaystyle=\mathcal{L}(\bar{\mathbf{Y}}_{r}\mid\bar{\mathbf{Y}}_{1:r-1},\mathbf{Q}_{1:r},\boldsymbol{\xi}_{1:r})+\log f(\mathbf{Q}_{r}\mid\mu_{q},\sigma_{q}^{2})+C_{1}

(C.72)

\displaystyle=\sum_{j=1}^{3}\bigg\{\bar{Y}_{r}^{(3j-2,3j-1)}\log(Q_{r}^{(3j-2,3j-1)}L_{r}^{(3j-2,3j-1)})

\displaystyle-L_{r}^{(3j-2,3j-1)}Q_{r}^{(3j-2,3j-1)}-\bar{Y}_{r}^{(3j-2,3j-1)}!

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{2}\left(\frac{Q_{r}^{(3j-2,3j-1)}-\mu_{q}}{\sigma_{q}}\right)^{2}\bigg\}+C_{2}

To get the mean of a Laplace approximation to the above we must find it’s maximum w.r.t.
\mathbf{Q}_{r}
, hence for
j=1,2,3
:

\displaystyle\frac{d\log\hat{p}(\mathbf{Q}_{r}\mid\mathbf{y}_{r})}{dQ_{r}^{(3j-2,3j-1)}}

\displaystyle=\frac{\bar{Y}_{r}^{(3j-2,3j-1)}}{Q_{r}^{(3j-2,3j-1)}}-L_{r}^{(3j-2,3j-1)}-\frac{Q_{r}^{(3j-2,3j-1)}-\mu_{q}}{\sigma_{q}^{2}}=0

(C.73)

\displaystyle\iff(Q_{r}^{(3j-2,3j-1)})^{2}+(L_{r}^{(3j-2,3j-1)}\sigma_{q}^{2}-\mu_{q})Q_{r}^{(3j-2,3j-1)}-\bar{Y}_{r}^{(3j-2,3j-1)}\sigma_{q}^{2}=0

\displaystyle\implies Q_{r}^{(3j-2,3j-1)}=\frac{1}{2}\left(\mu_{q}-L_{r}^{(3j-2,3j-1)}\sigma_{q}^{2}+\sqrt{(L_{r}^{(3j-2,3j-1)}\sigma_{q}^{2}-\mu_{q})^{2}+4\bar{Y}_{r}^{(3j-2,3j-1)}\sigma_{q}^{2}}\right)

\displaystyle=:\mu^{(j)}_{r}.

For the variance we find the second derivative and evaluate it at
\mu^{(j)}_{r}
:

\displaystyle\frac{d^{2}\log\hat{p}(\mathbf{q}_{r}\mid\mathbf{y}_{r})}{d(Q_{r}^{(3j-2,3j-1)})^{2}}=-\frac{\bar{Y}_{r}^{(3j-2,3j-1)}}{(Q_{r}^{(3j-2,3j-1)})^{2}}-\frac{1}{\sigma_{q}^{2}}

(C.74)

\displaystyle\implies\left(\sigma_{r}^{(j)}\right)^{2}=\left(\frac{\bar{Y}_{r}^{(3j-2,3j-1)}}{\left(\mu^{(j)}_{r}\right)^{2}}+\frac{1}{\sigma_{q}^{2}}\right)^{-1}.

Hence, having proposed
\xi_{r}
from its prior, we propose
\mathbf{Q}_{r}
by setting all elements to be zero except:

\displaystyle Q_{r}^{(3j-2,3j-1)}\sim\mathcal{N}\left(\mu_{r}^{(j)},(\sigma_{r}^{(j)})^{2}\right)_{\geq 0,\leq 1}\quad\quad\text{ for }j=1,2,3.

(C.75)

Let
\pi(\cdot\mid\bar{\mathbf{Y}}_{1:r},\mathbf{Q}_{1:r-1},\xi_{1:r})
be the proposal density associated with (
C.75
).

Algorithm 9

PAL within SMC for model of Rotavirus

1:

initialize:

\bar{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}_{0}^{(i)}\leftarrow\boldsymbol{\lambda}_{0}
for
i=1
to
n_{part}
.

2:

for

r\geq 1
:

3:

for

i=1
to
n_{part}

4:

\xi_{r}^{(i)}\sim\mathrm{Gamma}(\sigma_{\xi},\sigma_{\xi})

5:

for

t=\tau_{r-1}+1,\dots,\tau_{r}-1
:

\boldsymbol{\Lambda}_{t}^{(i)}\leftarrow((\bar{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}^{(i)}_{t-1}\odot\boldsymbol{\delta}_{t})\otimes\mathbf{1}_{m})\odot\mathbf{K}_{t,\boldsymbol{\eta}\left(\bar{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}_{t-1}^{(i)}\odot\boldsymbol{\delta}_{t}\right),\xi_{r}^{(i)}}+\boldsymbol{\alpha}_{t}

7:

\bar{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}^{(i)}_{t}\leftarrow(\mathbf{1}_{m}^{\top}\boldsymbol{\Lambda}_{t}^{(i)})^{\top}

for
i=1
to
n_{part}

resample

\left\{\bar{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}^{(i)}_{\tau_{r}}\right\}_{i=1}^{n_{part}}
according to a systematic resampling scheme with weights
\{w_{r}^{(i)}\}_{i=1}^{n_{part}}

20:

end for

The resulting approximate likelihood estimate for algorithm
9
is:

p(\bar{\mathbf{Y}}_{1:R})\approx\sum_{r=1}^{R}\hat{\mathcal{L}}(\bar{\mathbf{Y}}_{r}|\bar{\mathbf{Y}}_{1:r-1}).

(C.76)

Convergence plots for coordinate ascent algorithm

For each of EqEq EqOv, and OvOv, we performed a finite differencing coordinate ascent optimisation. That is, for each paramter: fix all others to their current value and approximate the sign of the gradient with finite differencing and take a step in positive gradient direction - cycle through parameters until convergence. Figures
24
,
25
, and
26
demonstrate the convergence of this procedure for each model EqEq, EqOv, and OvOv respectively.

Figure 24:
Plots showing 100 runs of the optimization procedure the EqEq rotavirus model applied to real data.

Figure 25:
Plots showing 100 runs of the optimization procedure the EqOv rotavirus model applied to real data.

Figure 26:
Plots showing 100 runs of the optimization procedure the OvOv rotavirus model applied to real data.

C.5.3
Rotavirus ARMA comparison details.

The benchmark model consists of an ARMA(2,0,1) model fit to the series
\log(cases+1)
independently for each age group, taking care to apply the appropriate jacobian transform to the likelihood.

C.6
Supplementary material for section
7.4

C.6.1
Model

As in
[
71
]
and
[
55
]
, we assume that
\beta_{r,k}
follows the school year:

\beta_{r,k}=\begin{cases}(1+2(1-p)a)\bar{\beta}_{k},&\text{during school term,}\\
(1-2pa)\bar{\beta}_{k},&\text{during school holidays,}\end{cases}

where
p=0.759
is the proportion of the year taken up by school terms,
\bar{\beta}_{k}>0
is the mean transition rate for city
k
, and
a
is the relative effect of holidays on transmission. Finally, the new infected and new removed are:

C_{k,t}\sim\text{Bin}(E_{k,t}-F_{k,t}^{(E)},1-e^{-h\rho}),\qquad D_{k,t}\sim\text{Bin}(I_{k,t}-F_{k,t}^{(I)},1-e^{-h\gamma}),

with
h/\penalty\rho
mean time spent in the exposed compartment and
h/\penalty\gamma
mean recovery time.
Given the vectors
\boldsymbol{\delta}_{k,t}=\left[\delta_{k,t}^{(S)}\;\delta_{k,t}^{(E)}\;\delta_{k,t}^{(I)}\;\delta_{k,t}^{(R)}\right]^{\top}\in\mathbb{R}^{4}_{\geq 0}
and
\boldsymbol{\alpha}_{k,t}=\left[\alpha_{k,t}^{(1)}\;0\;0\;0\right]^{\top}\in\mathbb{R}^{4}_{\geq 0}
, we have:

F^{(\cdot)}_{k,t}\sim\text{Bin}\left(\cdot_{k,t},1-\delta_{k,t}^{(\cdot)}\right),\qquad A_{k,t}\sim\text{Pois}\left(\alpha_{k,t}^{(1)}\right),

modelling the new births (immigration) into the susceptible population and the deaths (emigration) across compartments. Since there is no reinfection mechanism in the model (a realistic assumption for measles modelling), it is important to have new individuals enter the population to model the recurrent epidemic peaks present in the data. As already mentioned, for the model to capture recurrent peaks, it must accommodate recruitment into the susceptible compartments. Birthrate data for each city of the model is used to do this — as in
[
71
]
— it is assumed that newborns enter the susceptible class after a delay of
4
years, corresponding to the age an individual enters the high-risk school-age demographic. There is a further ‘cohort’ effect aspect to the model: it is assumed that at the start of the school year, a fraction
c\in(0,1)
of the lagged births enter the susceptible compartment, the remaining
1-c
proportion enter at a constant rate throughout the year. This informs the assumed rate parameters
\boldsymbol{\alpha}_{k,t}=[\alpha_{k,t}^{(1)}\;0\;0\;0\;]^{\top}
and, similarly, death rate records inform choice of
\boldsymbol{\delta}_{k,t}
. The values used for
\boldsymbol{\alpha}_{k,t}
and
\boldsymbol{\delta}_{k,t}
are reported in the data available on the GitHub page.

The observations are aggregated incidence data in the form of cumulative fortnightly transitions from infective to recovered for each of the
40
cities subject to binomial under-reporting, at times
\tau_{r}=4r
for
r=1,\dots,R
. Observations are modelled as transitions from infective to recovered compartments because, on discovery, cases are treated with bed rest and hence removed from the population
[
55
]
. Denoting observations as
\bar{\mathbf{Y}}_{k,r}=\sum_{t=\tau_{r-1}+1}^{\tau_{r}}\mathbf{Y}_{k,t}
where each
\mathbf{Y}_{k,t}\in\mathbb{N}^{4\times 4}
has each element equal to zero except for the
(3,4)
th element which, conditional on
D_{k,t}
, is distributed:

Y_{k,t}^{(3,4)}\sim\text{Bin}\left(D_{k,t},Q_{k,r}^{(3,4)}\right),\text{ for }t=\tau_{r-1},\dots\tau_{r},r\geq 1,\quad k=1,\dots,J,

where
\mathbf{Q}_{k,r}\in[0,1]^{4\times 4}
consists of all zeros apart from the
(3,4)
th entry, which is the reporting rate of transitions from infective to recovered. We assume that this rate follows
Q_{k,r}^{(3,4)}\sim\mathcal{N}(\mu_{q,k},\sigma_{q}^{2})_{\geq 0,\leq 1}
for
k=1,\dots,K
, denote this density with
f(\cdot\mid\cdot)
for the purposes of algorithm
10
. The mean under-reporting rate parameters,
\mu_{q,k}\in[0,1],k=1,\dots,J
, are assumed known for each city and are set to the same values as
[
55
]
, which are available in the data on the GitHub page,
\sigma_{q}^{2}>0
is to be estimated.

C.6.2
Inference

To employ the algorithms described in the methodology section we need to specify the transition matrix
\mathbf{K}_{r,\boldsymbol{\eta}}
, which in the case of this model is of size
4J\times 4J
. To be more succinct, we can write out a matrix
\mathbf{K}_{k,r,\tilde{\boldsymbol{\eta}}}
for each city
k=1,\dots,40
.
We define our matrices
\mathbf{K}_{r,\tilde{\boldsymbol{\eta}},\xi,k}
:

\mathbf{K}_{r,\boldsymbol{\eta},\xi,k}=\left[\begin{array}[]{cccc}e^{-hg_{k}(\beta_{k,r},\boldsymbol{\eta},\xi)}&1-e^{-hg_{k}(\beta_{k,r},\boldsymbol{\eta},\xi)}&0&0\\
0&e^{-h\rho}&1-e^{-h\rho}&0\\
0&0&e^{-h\gamma}&1-e^{-h\gamma}\\
0&0&0&1\end{array}\right],

where

g_{k}(\beta,\boldsymbol{\eta},\xi)=\beta\xi\cdot\left[{\eta}^{(k)}+\sum_{l\neq k}\frac{v_{kl}}{n_{k}}\left\{{\eta}^{(l)}-{\eta}^{(k)}\right\}\right],

One can identify matrices:

\mathbf{Z}_{k,t}:=\left[\begin{array}[]{cccc}S_{k,t}-F^{(S)}_{k,t}-B_{k,t}&B_{k,t}&0&0\\
0&E_{k,t}-F^{(E)}_{k,t}-C_{k,t}&C_{k,t}&0\\
0&0&I_{k,t}-F^{(I)}_{k,t}-D_{k,t}&D_{k,t}\\
0&0&0&R_{k,t}-F^{(R)}_{k,t}\end{array}\right],

and let
\mathbf{Z}_{t}
be block-diagonal with blocks
\mathbf{Z}_{k,t}
,
k=1,\ldots,J
. One can take advantage of the block-diagonal structure of
\mathbf{K}_{t,\boldsymbol{\eta}}
to implement an efficient block particle filter, see
[
56
]
and
[
52
]
, with lookahead resampling scheme
[
46
]
.

Proposals for algorithm
10

The details for the derivation of the proposals used in algorithm
10
lines
11
and
29
are similar to those of section
C.5
, so we omit them. Suppressing dependence on the particle, let
\mathbf{L}_{r,k}=\sum_{t=\tau_{r-1}+1}^{\tau_{r}}\boldsymbol{\Lambda}_{t,k}
with
\boldsymbol{\Lambda}_{t,k}
calculated as per line
7
(resp.
25
) of algorithm
9
and define:

\displaystyle\hat{\mu}_{r,k}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\left(\mu_{q,k}-L_{r,k}^{(3,4)}\sigma_{q}^{2}+\sqrt{(L_{r,k}^{(3,4)}\sigma_{q}^{2}-\mu_{q,k})^{2}+4\bar{Y}_{r}^{(3,4)}\sigma_{q}^{2}}\right),

(C.77)

\displaystyle\left(\hat{\sigma}_{r,k}\right)^{2}

\displaystyle=\left(\frac{\bar{Y}_{r}^{(3,4)}}{\left(\hat{\mu}_{r,k}\right)^{2}}+\frac{1}{\sigma_{q}^{2}}\right)^{-1}.

(C.78)

Then in line
11
(resp.
29
) we make the proposals:

Q_{k,r}^{(3,4)}\sim\mathcal{N}\left(\hat{\mu}_{r,k},\hat{\sigma}_{r,k}^{2}\right)_{\geq 0,\leq 1}.

(C.79)

Algorithm 10

PAL within a lookahead block particle filter

1:

initialize:

\bar{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}_{0,k}^{(i)}\leftarrow n_{k,0}\boldsymbol{\pi}_{k,0}
set
\log\zeta_{0,k}^{(i)}\leftarrow 0
and set
\log W_{0,k}^{(i)}\leftarrow 0
for
i=1
to
n_{part}
and
k=1,\dots,K.

for

r\geq 1
:

3:

for

:

4:

for

5:

\xi_{k,r}^{(i)}\sim\mathrm{Gamma}(\sigma_{\xi},\sigma_{\xi})

6:

for

\mathbf{Q}_{k,r}^{(i)}\sim\pi\left(\cdot\mid\left\{\boldsymbol{\Lambda}^{(i)}_{t,k}\right\}_{t=\tau_{r-1}+1}^{\tau_{r}},\bar{\mathbf{Y}}_{r,k},\boldsymbol{\varphi}\right)
as per

13:

\mathcal{L}(\bar{\mathbf{Y}}_{r,k}|\bar{\mathbf{Y}}_{1:r-1,k})\leftarrow-\mathbf{1}_{m}^{\top}\mathbf{M}_{r,k}^{(i)}\mathbf{1}_{m}+\mathbf{1}_{m}^{\top}\left(\bar{\mathbf{Y}}_{r,k}\odot\log\mathbf{M}_{r,k}^{(i)}\right)\mathbf{1}_{m}-\mathbf{1}_{m}^{\top}\left(\log\bar{\mathbf{Y}}_{r,k}!\right)\mathbf{1}_{m}

\small\log w^{(i)}_{r,k}\leftarrow\mathcal{L}(\bar{\mathbf{Y}}_{r,k}|\bar{\mathbf{Y}}_{1:r-1,k})+\log\left(f(\mathbf{Q}_{k,r}^{(i)}\mid\mathbf{Q}_{k,1:r-1}^{(i)})\right)-\log\left(\pi\left(\mathbf{Q}_{k,r}^{(i)}\mid\left\{\boldsymbol{\Lambda}^{(i)}_{t,k}\right\}_{t=\tau_{r-1}+1}^{\tau_{r}},\bar{\mathbf{Y}}_{r,k},\boldsymbol{\varphi}\right)\right)

15:

\bar{W}_{r-1,k}^{(i)}\leftarrow W_{r-1,k}^{(i)}/\sum_{j}W_{r-1,k}^{(j)}
for
i=1
to
n_{part}

16:

\log W_{r,k}^{(i)}\leftarrow\log\bar{W}_{r-1,k}^{(i)}+\log w_{r,k}^{(i)}

17:

\bar{\boldsymbol{\Lambda}}^{(i)}_{\tau_{r},k}\leftarrow(\mathbf{1}_{m}\otimes\mathbf{1}_{m}-\mathbf{Q}_{k,r}^{(i)})\odot\boldsymbol{\Lambda}_{\tau_{r},k}+\bar{\mathbf{Y}}_{{r,k}}\odot\boldsymbol{\Lambda}_{\tau_{r},k}^{(i)}\odot\mathbf{Q}_{k,r}^{(i)}\oslash\mathbf{M}_{r,k}^{(i)}

18:

\bar{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}^{(i)}_{\tau_{r},k}\leftarrow(\mathbf{1}_{m}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{\Lambda}}^{(i)}_{\tau_{r},k})^{\top}+\boldsymbol{\alpha}_{\tau_{r},k}

19:

end for

20:

end for

21:

for

k=1,\dots,J
:

22:

for

i=1,\dots,n_{part}

23:

\tilde{\xi}_{k,r+1}^{(i)}\sim\mathrm{Gamma}(\sigma_{\xi},\sigma_{\xi})

24:

for

t=\tau_{r}+1,\dots,\tau_{r+1}-1
:

25:

\boldsymbol{\Lambda}_{t,k}^{(i)}\leftarrow((\bar{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}^{(i)}_{t-1,k}\odot\boldsymbol{\delta}_{t,k})\otimes\mathbf{1}_{m})\odot\mathbf{K}_{r+1,\boldsymbol{\eta}\left(\bar{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}_{\tau_{r},1:J}^{(i)}\right),\tilde{\xi}_{r+1,k}^{(i)},k}

26:

\bar{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}^{(i)}_{t,k}\leftarrow(\mathbf{1}_{m}^{\top}\boldsymbol{\Lambda}_{t,k}^{(i)})^{\top}+\boldsymbol{\alpha}_{t,k}

27:

end for

28:

\boldsymbol{\Lambda}^{(i)}_{\tau_{r+1},k}\leftarrow((\boldsymbol{\lambda}^{(i)}_{\tau_{r+1}-1,k}\odot\boldsymbol{\delta}_{\tau_{r+1},k})\otimes\mathbf{1}_{m})\odot\mathbf{K}_{r+1,\boldsymbol{\eta}\left(\bar{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}_{\tau_{r-1},1:J}^{(i)}\right),\tilde{\xi}_{r+1,k}^{(i)},k}

29:

\tilde{\mathbf{Q}}_{k,r+1}^{(i)}\sim\pi\left(\cdot\mid\left\{\boldsymbol{\Lambda}^{(i)}_{t,k}\right\}_{t=\tau_{r}+1}^{\tau_{r+1}},\bar{\mathbf{Y}}_{r+1,k},\boldsymbol{\varphi}\right)
as per
C.79

30:

\mathbf{M}_{r+1,k}^{(i)}\leftarrow\sum_{t=\tau_{r}+1}^{\tau_{r+1}}\boldsymbol{\Lambda}^{(i)}_{t,k}\odot\tilde{\mathbf{Q}}_{r+1,k}^{(i)}

31:

\mathcal{L}(\bar{\mathbf{Y}}_{r+1,k}|\bar{\mathbf{Y}}_{1:r,k})\leftarrow-\mathbf{1}_{m}^{\top}\mathbf{M}_{r+1,k}^{(i)}\mathbf{1}_{m}+\mathbf{1}_{m}^{\top}\left(\bar{\mathbf{Y}}_{r+1,k}\odot\log\mathbf{M}_{r+1,k}^{(i)}\right)\mathbf{1}_{m}-\mathbf{1}_{m}^{\top}\left(\log\bar{\mathbf{Y}}_{r+1,k}!\right)\mathbf{1}_{m}

32:

\small\log w^{(i)}_{r+1,k}\leftarrow\mathcal{L}(\bar{\mathbf{Y}}_{r+1,k}|\bar{\mathbf{Y}}_{1:r,k})+\log\left(f(\mathbf{Q}_{k,r+1}^{(i)}\mid\mathbf{Q}_{k,1:r}^{(i)})\right)-\log\left(\pi\left(\mathbf{Q}_{k,r+1}^{(i)}\mid\left\{\boldsymbol{\Lambda}^{(i)}_{t,k}\right\}_{t=\tau_{r}+1}^{\tau_{r+1}},\bar{\mathbf{Y}}_{r+1,k},\boldsymbol{\varphi}\right)\right)

33:

\log\zeta_{r,k}^{(i)}\leftarrow\log W_{r,k}^{(i)}+\log w_{r+1,k}^{(i)}

34:

end for

35:

\hat{\mathcal{L}}(\bar{\mathbf{Y}}_{r,k}|\bar{\mathbf{Y}}_{1:r-1,k})\leftarrow\log\left(\sum_{j}W_{r,k}^{(j)}\right)

36:

\bar{\zeta}_{r,k}^{(i)}\leftarrow\zeta_{r,k}^{(i)}/\sum_{j}\zeta_{r,k}^{(j)}
for
i=1
to
n_{part}

37:

resample

\left\{\bar{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}^{(i)}_{\tau_{r},k},W_{r,k}^{(i)},\zeta_{r,k}^{(i)}\right\}_{i=1}^{n_{part}}
with weights
\{\bar{\zeta}_{r,k}^{(i)}\}_{i=1}^{n_{part}}

38:

\log W_{r,k}^{(i)}\leftarrow\log W_{r,k}^{(i)}-\log\zeta_{r,k}^{(i)}

39:

end for

Inference

In each model instance, A,B, and C, described in
7.4
, we can define
\boldsymbol{\vartheta}
,
\bar{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{1:T}
, and
\boldsymbol{\varphi}
:

•

A:
\boldsymbol{\vartheta}=\left[\boldsymbol{\pi}_{0}\;\bar{\beta}\;\rho\;\gamma\;g\;a\;c\right]
,
\left\{\bar{\boldsymbol{\theta}_{r}}\right\}_{r\geq 0}=\left\{\left[\xi_{1,r}\;\dots\;\xi_{40,r}\;Q_{1,r}^{(3,4)}\;\dots\;Q_{40,r}^{(3,4)}\right]\right\}_{r\geq 0}
, and
\boldsymbol{\varphi}=[\sigma_{q}^{2},\sigma_{\xi}]
.

•

B:
\boldsymbol{\vartheta}=\left[\boldsymbol{\pi}_{1,0}\;\dots\;\boldsymbol{\pi}_{40,0}\;\bar{\beta}\;\rho\;\gamma\;g\;a\;c\right]
,
\left\{\bar{\boldsymbol{\theta}_{r}}\right\}_{r\geq 0}=\left\{\left[\xi_{1,r},\dots,\xi_{40,r},Q_{1,r}^{(3,4)},\dots,Q_{40,r}^{(3,4)}\right]\right\}_{r\geq 0}
, and
\boldsymbol{\varphi}=[\sigma_{q}^{2},\sigma_{\xi}]
.

•

C:
\boldsymbol{\vartheta}=\left[\boldsymbol{\pi}_{1,0}\;\dots\;\boldsymbol{\pi}_{40,0}\;\bar{\beta}_{1}\;\dots\;\bar{\beta}_{40}\;\rho\;\gamma\;g\;a\;c\right]
,
\left\{\bar{\boldsymbol{\theta}_{r}}\right\}_{r\geq 0}=\left\{\left[\xi_{1,r},\dots,\xi_{40,r},Q_{1,r}^{(3,4)},\dots,Q_{40,r}^{(3,4)}\right]\right\}_{r\geq 0}
, and
\boldsymbol{\varphi}=[\sigma_{q}^{2},\sigma_{\xi}]
.

Each block, labelled
k=1,\dots,J
, corresponds to a specific city. This block structure allows one to perform proposals and weighting locally to each block, avoiding explicit high-dimensional filtering. At time
r
, the lookahead scheme consists of: performing a ‘regular’ particle propagation and reweighting step (the usual SMC iteration), then we propagate again each particle and run a PAL iteration for time
r+1
, with ‘dummy’ particles (used purely for weighting purposes, denoted with tildes in algorithm
10
), we then weight the original particles proportionally to the joint likelihood of the regular and dummy particles at times
r
and
r+1
- taking care to apply the appropriate correction in the likelihood calculation, dummy particles are then discarded. We found that this scheme greatly reduced Monte Carlo error. See algorithm
10
for our implementation.The resulting approximate log-likelihood estimate associated with algorithm
10
is:

\log p(\bar{\mathbf{Y}}_{1:J,1:R})\approx\sum_{r=1}^{R}\sum_{k=1}^{J}\hat{\mathcal{L}}(\bar{\mathbf{Y}}_{r,k}|\bar{\mathbf{Y}}_{1:r-1,k}).

(C.80)

The optimisation scheme we used is described in figure
27
. We report the inferences for model
C
in table
9
.

C.6.3
Measles ARMA comparison details.

The benchmark model consists of an ARMA(2,0,1) model fit to the series
\log(cases+1)
independently for each each city, taking care to apply the appropriate jacobian transform to the likelihood.

Measles projection details.

The sample, size
300
, of projected case numbers used to produce figure
6
in the main article were generated by the following workflow:

1.

Presampling
\xi_{k,r}^{(i)}\sim\text{Gamma}(\sigma_{\xi},\sigma_{\xi})
with
\sigma_{\xi}
set to our point estimate, for
k=1,...,40
,
r=1,...,4
, and
i=1,\dots,300
.

2.

Running our PALSMC scheme on the original dataset with 300 particles and parameters set to our point estimates, taking as output a sample of final time-point population state intensity vectors
\bar{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}_{T,k}^{(i)}
.

3.

For
i=1,\dots,300
and
k=1,\dots,40
, propagate the intensity vectors through the transition kernel using the iteration for
t=1,\dots,16
(corresponding to 8 weeks):

\displaystyle\boldsymbol{\Lambda}_{t,k}^{(i)}

\displaystyle=((\bar{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}^{(i)}_{t-1,k}\odot\boldsymbol{\delta}_{t,k})\otimes\mathbf{1}_{m})\odot\mathbf{K}_{r,\boldsymbol{\eta}\left(\bar{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}_{\tau_{r-1},1:J}^{(i)}\right),\xi_{r,k}^{(i)},k}

(C.81)

\displaystyle\bar{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}^{(i)}_{t,k}

\displaystyle=(\mathbf{1}_{m}^{\top}\boldsymbol{\Lambda}_{t,k}^{(i)})^{\top}+\boldsymbol{\alpha}_{t,k}

(C.82)

Where
\boldsymbol{\alpha}_{t,k}
and
\boldsymbol{\delta}_{t,k}
are chosen according to the assumption that birth rates and death rates remain constant.

4.

Simulate
I^{(i)}_{k,t}\sim\text{Pois}(\bar{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}^{(i)}_{t,k})
for
t
corresponding to weeks
2,4,6,
and
8
for each sample
i=1,\dots,300
.

Figure 27:
Approximate log-likelihood values for the measles data under scenarios A, B, and C. For each scenario, the optimal combination of parameters was obtained through Sequential Least Squares Programming (SLSQP) with target function given by algorithm
10
with 5000 particles and lookahead resampling, this scheme was initialised randomly at 100 points over feasible values, we present the best attained values. After the optimization, algorithm
10
with 5000 particles and lookahead resampling is run 100 times on the optimized parameters to build the boxplots and estimate the variance of the approximate log-likelihood.
